## Session 9 (2026-08-19) — List/Create/View PO redesigned to the design system; FIFO costing decided for the future Work Order module

Continuation of Session 8's design-system rollout, still on branch `redesign`, still **uncommitted**. Two unrelated threads this session: (1) finished applying `design-system/mims/MASTER.md` to the pages Session 8 didn't touch, with several real design decisions along the way, not just a token find-replace; (2) a backend architecture discussion (no code written — guide-only, per standing preference) that locked in FIFO as the inventory costing method for the not-yet-built Work Order/Production module.

### List PO redesign
- **Status filter replaced with tabs + live counts**, dropping the old `<flux:select>`. Status is the page's central axis, so it now gets first-class UI — "All (42) · Draft (5) · Pending Approval (3) · ..." as clickable pills (`wire:click="$set('status', '...')"`), counts scoped to the active search term only (not the currently-selected status), so every tab always shows what it *would* contain if clicked. Required splitting the component's `filteredQuery()` into a `searchFilteredQuery()` (search only) + `filteredQuery()` (search + status), so the new `statusCounts()` computed property can reuse the search predicate without inheriting the status filter.
- **Responsive card view added** (`lg:hidden`) — the 7-column table would force horizontal scroll below `lg`; built a genuinely different stacked-card layout instead of shrinking the table, first instance of this pattern in the app.
- **Loading feedback** (`wire:loading` + accent spinner + "Updating...") added on filter changes, matching the pattern already established on Create PO.
- **Late-delivery indicator redesigned**, at the user's request (disliked plain red text). Landed on a small chip — `bg-red-50`/`dark:bg-red-500/10` background, border, a `clock` icon, the date — reusing the exact recipe already established for the Low-stock warning elsewhere in the app, so it reads as a familiar "flag" rather than a new pattern. Satisfies MASTER.md's own `color-not-only` rule (icon+shape carry the signal, not hue alone). Worth knowing: this shares the red family with the `Rejected` status badge elsewhere in the row — low collision risk in practice since a `Rejected` PO is excluded from the overdue check via `isTerminal()`, but flagged in case it ever reads as confusing.
- **Density pass**: table body `text-sm`→`text-xs`, `py-2.5`→`py-2`, at the user's request that the row text felt too large next to the `text-[10px]` header.
- **Money weight de-bolded**: every `font-semibold`/`font-bold` on a money figure (row Total, mobile card Total, both figures in the bottom summary bar) dropped to `font-medium` with full-contrast color (`text-zinc-900 dark:text-white` instead of `zinc-800`/`zinc-200`) instead of relying on weight — user specifically disliked bold subtotals. Emphasis now comes from contrast, not weight. Same treatment then applied to Create PO's per-row Subtotal and Est. Total panel (bumped `text-lg`→`text-xl` there since it had room, being the single most important figure on that page).
- `font-mono` → `font-data` (Fira Code token) on all money/date/code cells, consistent with MASTER.md's typography table.
- 4 new strings added to `lang/id.json` ("All", "Updating...", "Clear filters", "No delivery date").

### View PO — full redesign
- Header gained the eyebrow ("Purchasing · Order Detail") + accent underline motif shared with Login/Create PO/List. Fixed a pre-existing bug in passing: the `<h1>` had both `font-display` and `font-mono` classes fighting each other (undefined cascade winner) — now just `font-data`, correct for a PO number (a code, not a title phrase).
- **Order Summary card reworked**: the light accent-wash background (`bg-accent/[0.04]`) was replaced with the `border-s-accent` left-border treatment used elsewhere, and the Total figure moved into a solid `bg-accent` block mirroring Create PO's Est. Total panel — same object, same treatment, across both pages. Then a follow-up fix: in the 3-column info-card grid, the Supplier/Schedule cards are taller (more rows) than Order Summary, and CSS Grid stretches all three to match row height — but the accent Total block wasn't anchored to the bottom, so it sat right under "Line Items" with dead space beneath it. Fixed with `flex flex-col h-full` on the card + `mt-auto` on the accent block, so Total now always sits flush with the card's bottom edge regardless of sibling height.
- **Late-delivery chip** (same recipe as List PO) added to the Schedule card's Expected Delivery row — this page didn't have any overdue handling before at all.
- **Responsive mobile card view added for the line-items table** — same "no horizontal scroll below `lg`" reasoning as List PO, but harder here since this table has live inline editing (Qty/Unit Price inputs, Save/Delete, an Add-Item row), not just display. Built a full-parity `lg:hidden` stacked-card alternative that keeps all editing capability, not a read-only fallback — this page's whole purpose is editing, so a read-only mobile view would have been a functional regression, not just a visual one.
- Density pass + `font-data` + money de-bolding applied consistently with List PO (table `text-sm`→`text-xs`/`py-2.5`→`py-2`, per-row Subtotal `font-semibold`→`font-medium` + full contrast).
- 3 new strings added to `lang/id.json` ("Order Detail", "Add Item", "Save changes").

### Costing method decided: FIFO (for the future Work Order/Production module)
User confirmed the Cost Accounting module (see "Future module note" above) will need to extract **material cost** per production run once Work Orders exist — asked whether the current PO/stock setup supports that. Verified against the actual migrations (not from memory) before answering: `purchase_order_items.unit_price` is real acquisition-cost data, but `stock_movements.amount` is a plain `decimal(12,2)` **quantity**, no cost/currency semantics at all. Once stock is received, it pools into one `products.current_stock` number — there's no batch/lot memory of which PO a given unit of stock came from, so a future Work Order's `stock_movements` "out" row (material consumed) currently has no way to answer "at what cost."

**Two costing approaches were discussed; user chose FIFO over the simpler Weighted Average Cost (WAC) option:**
- **WAC** would have been a single cached `products.average_cost` column, recalculated on every `in` movement (weighted by quantity) — cheap, fits the existing "cached/derived field, recalculated under `lockForUpdate()`" pattern used for `current_stock`/`total_amount` elsewhere. Rejected in favor of accuracy.
- **FIFO** (chosen) requires real batch/lot tracking since consumption must draw from the oldest batch first, potentially splitting a single consumption event across multiple differently-priced batches. This needs **two new tables**, not built yet:
  - **`stock_lots`** — one row per batch that ever entered inventory: `product_id`, a link back to the `stock_movements` row that created it, `unit_cost`, `quantity_received` (immutable), `quantity_remaining` (mutable, decremented as FIFO draws from it), `received_at` (defines draw order). Conceptually this is a *mutable balance* table (same category as `products.current_stock`), not an *immutable ledger* like `stock_movements` — a genuinely new "kind" of table for this codebase, though consistent with the existing cached-balance pattern.
  - **An allocation table** (e.g. `stock_movement_lot_allocations`) — records exactly which lot(s) a given `out` movement drew from and how much, since one movement can span multiple lots at different costs. Without this, a `stock_movements` row alone can't answer its own cost once it spans more than one lot.
- **Sequencing decision:** lot *creation* (the `in` side) should be added to `CreateStockMovementAction`'s existing `in` path once this is picked back up — cheap relative to rebuilding it later. Lot *consumption/draw-down* (the FIFO walk, `out` side) should wait until the Production/Work Order module actually exists to call it, consistent with the project's standing "don't scaffold untestable pieces" rule (same reasoning already applied to the low-stock notification in Session 6).
- **⚠️ Correction/risk flagged after re-reading this file, not caught during the live discussion:** `CreateStockMovementAction` and `ReceivePurchaseOrderAction` are **already written and in use** (Sessions 7–8), including real dev/test data (the `warehouse@test.com` manual-testing account). This means real `stock_movements` "in" rows almost certainly already exist **without** a corresponding lot. Adding lot-creation to the Action now does not retroactively backfill those — when FIFO consumption logic is eventually built, any stock that predates the lot-tracking change will need to be treated as one lump "opening balance" lot (a normal ERP-migration pattern, not a blocker, but a real decision to make explicitly rather than discover as a bug).
- **Explicitly deferred, not solved:** what "cost" means for a `ProductionOutput` movement (finished goods entering inventory) — not a purchase price, but a rollup of that Work Order's own material+labor+overhead cost. Fully blocked on the Work Order feature itself existing. Also deferred: whether `stock_movements` gets its own cached `total_cost` (summed from that movement's lot allocations) for reporting convenience — likely yes, same "cache the derived total" precedent as `subtotal`/`total_amount`, but only worth adding once the allocation table exists.

## Session 10 (2026-08-22/23) — `views/layouts` reverted to default component path (+ a real bug it caused, fixed); long Livewire-internals teaching thread

Opened by asking where things left off — surfaced the staleness note above (branch is now `receive_po`, handover was still narrating `redesign`/Session 9). Not resolved this session; got pulled into a different, concrete request instead.

### `resources/views/layouts` → `resources/views/components/layouts` revert
User recalled asking, in an earlier unlogged session, for `Blade::anonymousComponentPath(resource_path('views/layouts'), 'layouts')` to be registered in `AppServiceProvider::boot()` (this line predates the current git history — present since the very first commit) — layout files (`app.blade.php`, `app/sidebar.blade.php`, `app/header.blade.php`, `auth.blade.php`, `auth/{card,simple,split}.blade.php`, `guest.blade.php`) lived under `resources/views/layouts/`, referenced via the double-colon namespace syntax (`<x-layouts::app>`, `<x-layouts::app.sidebar>`, etc.). User asked to revert to Laravel's actual default: components under `resources/views/components/`.

Done via `git mv` (history-preserving) — files now at `resources/views/components/layouts/...`; every consuming file (`dashboard.blade.php`, `dashboards/placeholder.blade.php`, the 6 `pages/auth/*.blade.php` files, plus the layout files' own internal cross-references) updated from `x-layouts::x` to `x-layouts.x` (double-colon namespace syntax → default dot notation) via a bulk `sed` pass, verified with a follow-up grep (zero `x-layouts::` remaining anywhere in `resources/`). Removed the `Blade::anonymousComponentPath(...)` line and the now-unused `Blade` facade import from `AppServiceProvider.php`. Leftover now-empty `resources/views/layouts/{app,auth}/` directories (git doesn't track empty dirs, so `git mv` doesn't clean up the parent) removed manually.

### Real bug this caused — Livewire page-component layout wrapping broke
Verified (not assumed) that `view('layouts::app')` threw `InvalidArgumentException: No hint path defined for [layouts]` after the revert. Root cause: `Blade::anonymousComponentPath($path, $prefix)` does **two** things at once — registers the `<x-prefix::name>` component-tag prefix, *and* registers `prefix` as a plain Blade view namespace (`view('prefix::name')` lookups). Livewire's own vendor config (`vendor/livewire/livewire/config/livewire.php`, not published in this project until this session) ships `'component_layout' => 'layouts::app'` as its **default** — this is the view Livewire auto-wraps around any `Route::livewire(...)`-registered full-page component that doesn't declare its own `#[Layout(...)]`. That default silently depended on the exact line just deleted; deleting it broke every existing `Route::livewire()` page (Create/List/View PO, all 3 Settings pages) with no visible symptom until actually hit (confirmed via `view()->render()` in tinker, not from reading the config alone).

**Fixed** by running `php artisan vendor:publish --tag=livewire:config` (so `config/livewire.php` is now a real, version-controlled project file instead of an invisible vendor default) and changing `component_layout` to `'components.layouts.app'` — matches the new default component location, no custom Blade namespace involved anywhere in the app anymore. Verified fixed: same tinker render now gets all the way through layout+sidebar+user-menu rendering (only remaining error is "no authenticated user," expected/unavoidable outside a real HTTP request, same limitation noted since Session 4).

**Not yet done:** dashboards/placeholder pages (`Route::view(...)` targets) were never affected by any of this — they self-wrap with explicit `<x-layouts.app>` tags in their own templates and don't go through `component_layout` at all (see Concepts below). Nothing else to fix there.

### Long teaching thread — how Livewire page components actually render (guide/explanation only, no further code changes)
Extended back-and-forth, working from actual framework/package source (`vendor/livewire/livewire/src/Features/SupportPageComponents/*`, `vendor/livewire/livewire/src/Drawer/ImplicitRouteBinding.php`, `vendor/laravel/fortify/src/*`, `vendor/laravel/framework/.../Application.php`) rather than from memory throughout. Full list of what got covered lives in "Concepts Already Taught" below. One concrete, previously-unknown gap surfaced along the way and deliberately **not fixed** (flagged only): 2FA-completed logins go through a different Fortify contract (`TwoFactorLoginResponse`, resolved by `TwoFactorAuthenticatedSessionController::store()`) than plain password logins (`LoginResponse`, resolved by `AuthenticatedSessionController::store()`) — this app's `AppServiceProvider` only overrides `LoginResponseContract`, so a 2FA-completed login currently falls through to Fortify's generic `redirect()->intended(Fortify::redirects('login'))` instead of the role-based dashboard redirect. Confirmed via grep — nothing in `app/` references `TwoFactorLoginResponse`. Low priority until real 2FA users exist to hit this path.

## Session 11 (2026-09-08) — PO Receipt Detail UI built (assistant-written, per-request exception); full DB design for attachments + batch costing, guide-only

Branch is `main` now (`receive_po` was merged in — see the merge commit and "finish redesign"/"most logic done" commits git log already showed at session start; **these commits' actual contents still haven't been reconciled against this file**, same staleness gap Session 10 flagged and didn't resolve — still open, see the ⚠️ note above Session 10). Working tree at end of session has 3 uncommitted files: `lang/id.json`, `pages/auth/login.blade.php`, `pages/warehouse/inbound/purchasing/⚡detail.blade.php`.

### UI track — assistant wrote code directly to files this session, per explicit per-request instruction
The standing rule (2026-08-26 note above) is "assistant shows code, user types it" — that held for most of this session (login page redesign, most of the receiving-detail UI), but the user explicitly said "code it up for me" / "please write it for me" at several distinct points, and the assistant used Write/Edit directly for those specific requests. Flagging this as a **pattern observed, not a rule change**: the standing default is still show-don't-write; explicit per-message override requests should keep being honored in the moment without asking to renegotiate the whole rule.

**`pages/auth/login.blade.php`** — added full dark-mode variants (page was previously light-only despite the rest of the app supporting `.dark`), a loading spinner + `:disabled` state on submit (`x-data="{ loading: false }"`, Alpine), an icon-focuses-accent-color micro-interaction via Tailwind v4's `group-has-[:focus]:`, a Caps Lock warning on the password field (`@keyup`+`getModifierState`), and a shake animation on fields that already have a server-side validation error on page load.

**`pages/warehouse/inbound/purchasing/⚡detail.blade.php`** — this file already had `orderedQuantitySummary()`/`receivedQuantitySummary()` computed properties and a bare `{{ }}`-dumped Collection (from before this session); built out into a real page this session:
- **Receiving Summary card** — new `receivingProgress()` computed property (bcmath-based `%`, clamped `barPercentage` for the visual bar vs. uncapped `percentage` for the text so an over-received PO can't overflow the bar), progress bar animated via `transform: scaleX()` (not `width`, avoids layout reflow), `role="progressbar"` + `aria-value*` for a11y, green/checkmark state at 100%.
- **Items to Receive table** (+ `lg:hidden` mobile card fallback, matching the existing `purchasing/orders/show.blade.php` responsive convention) — Product/Ordered/Received/Remaining/Receive Now/Status columns. "Receive Now" is a real `flux:input.group` input per non-fully-received item (unit welded on as a suffix) — **UI only, no `wire:model`, no backing property** — explicitly deferred by the user until the DB design (below) is settled.
- **Attachments section** — drag-and-drop zone + file list, **pure Alpine client-side state** (`x-data="{ files: [] }"`) — no actual upload wiring yet, this is a visual mock the real Livewire `WithFileUploads` implementation will replace.
- **Actions bar** — Cancel (real link back to the PO list) + Submit Receipt (`type="button"`, does nothing yet).
- Fixed 3 pre-existing markup bugs noticed along the way while editing this section: a dangling `border-` typo, `bg-whitedark:bg-zinc-900` missing a space (so `dark:` never applied), and the outer summary `<div>` never being closed.

### Backend design track — guide-only discussion (2026-09-08), **nothing written**, this is what's queued for next session

Prompted by: attachments need mandatory proof-of-delivery files, and the user separately wants those receiving events to eventually feed **batch/lot costing into a future Production module** ("this batch cost will be used to determine total cost for 1 production batch").

**1. Delivery-visit grouping — `purchase_order_receipt_batches` + `purchase_order_receipt_attachments`.**
Problem: `purchase_order_receipts` is per-item (already existing schema, one row per `purchase_order_item_id`) — if a worker receives 3 different products in one visit and attaches 1 delivery-note photo, nothing in the schema represents "the visit" for that photo to belong to.
- `purchase_order_receipt_batches` — `purchase_order_id`, `received_by`, `received_at`, timestamps. One row per "Submit Receipt" click/one physical delivery visit.
- `purchase_order_receipts` gains a `batch_id` FK.
- `purchase_order_receipt_attachments` — `batch_id` FK (**not unique** — one batch can have many attachments), `disk`, `path`, `original_filename`, `mime_type`, `size`, timestamps. "Mandatory" = `count(attachments) >= 1` at submit time, checked in whatever orchestrating Action ties the item-loop + batch creation together (not written yet — needs a new Action, working name not yet decided, something like `SubmitPurchaseOrderReceiptBatchAction`).
- Why not skip the batch and attach straight to the PO: loses the ability to tell which attachment belongs to which visit once a PO is received across 2+ separate deliveries — a real, anticipated case given `PurchaseOrderStatus::PartiallyReceived` already exists specifically for it.

**2. File storage mechanics** (discussed, not yet built):
- **`local` disk** (private, `storage/app/private`), not `public` — this is an internal auth-gated app (MASTER.md), attachments are business documents, `public` disk would make them reachable by anyone with the URL, no auth check. Viewing/downloading later needs a route that runs the same PO-visibility auth check as the rest of the app.
- Upload mechanism is Livewire's `WithFileUploads` trait (not a plain form/controller upload) — native `<input type="file" wire:model="...">`, Livewire eagerly uploads to a temp location (`livewire-tmp/`, 24h auto-expiry) the moment a file is picked, permanent `store()` call happens inside the actual submit action.
- DB row per attachment needs `disk`/`path`/`original_filename`/`mime_type`/`size` — `path` is Laravel's auto-generated random path (never the user's raw filename, path-traversal/collision risk).
- Confirmed (via `.gitignore` + `git check-ignore`) that `storage/app/private` and `storage/app/public` are already correctly gitignored — uploads never reach the repo. Discussed production-deploy persistence separately: no CD pipeline exists in this repo yet (`.github/workflows/` is lint+tests only, no Docker/Forge/Envoyer config) — flagged for whenever deployment is actually set up: a direct `git pull`-on-server deploy is safe by default (gitignored files are untouched by `git pull`; just never run `git clean -fdx` or `rm -rf storage` in a deploy script), but a fresh-checkout-per-release tool (Forge/Envoyer-style) needs `storage/` explicitly symlinked from a shared persistent directory or every deploy starts it empty.

**3. Costing — `inventory_lots` (renamed from "batch" mid-discussion specifically to avoid colliding with #1's batch/delivery-visit meaning — these are two different concepts, keep the names distinct anywhere this gets written up or built).**
- User confirmed: (a) batch/lot cost is **derived**, not manually entered — `unit_cost = purchaseOrderItem.unit_price`, computed live via the relation chain, never stored as its own column. Safe against drift because `PurchaseOrderItem.unit_price` can only change while the PO is `Draft` (`ensureIsEditable()`), and lots only exist once receiving has started (`Approved`/`PartiallyReceived`), so by the time a lot exists its source price is already frozen. (b) Wants real FIFO/specific-lot tracking (not weighted-average) — explicitly because batch cost needs to trace through into "cost for 1 production batch" later, which only works if individual lots keep their identity rather than blending into one evolving average.
- `purchase_order_receipts` already is, structurally, the right unit for a "lot" (already one row per product per receiving event) — but rather than bolting `remaining_quantity` directly onto that table, the user's own instinct (validated as the better call) was a **separate** table: `purchase_order_receipts` stays a pure immutable historical record, `inventory_lots` owns the one number that's genuinely mutable over time.
- **`inventory_lots`**: `product_id` (**denormalized** — confirmed this session, see reasoning below), `purchase_order_receipt_id` (FK, **unique** — enforces one lot per item-receipt at the DB level), `remaining_quantity` (decimal, starts = the receipt's `quantity_received`, decremented later by production consumption), timestamps.
- **`product_id` denormalization reasoning**: the FIFO query this table exists for ("this product's lots with `remaining_quantity > 0`, oldest first") is the hot path, not a rare report — normalizing it out would mean `hasManyThrough` can't reach across the required 2 hops (`inventory_lots → purchase_order_receipts → purchase_order_items → products`), forcing nested `whereHas()` on every draw. Precedent already exists in this exact codebase: `stock_movements.product_id` is already a real stored column even though it also has a `nullableMorphs('reference')` that could theoretically be walked to find the product — same "store what you query by constantly, don't force a join through a reference chain" call already made once. Denormalization risk here is near-zero since `product_id` is write-once at lot creation (inside whatever Action creates the receipt+lot together), never updated afterward.
- FIFO consumption itself (draining `remaining_quantity`, splitting a single production draw across multiple lots if the oldest doesn't have enough) is explicitly **not** being built now — same "don't scaffold untestable pieces" stance already applied to the low-stock notification (Session 6) and lot *draw-down* (Session 9) — `remaining_quantity` just needs to exist now so there's no backfill migration once Production is built.
- **Where lot creation slots in**: alongside `PurchaseOrderReceipt` creation inside the existing `ReceivePurchaseOrderAction` (already written, Session 8) — one `InventoryLot` row per `PurchaseOrderReceipt` row, not a new orchestration layer for this specific piece.

**⚠️ Reconcile with Session 9 before writing any migration:** Session 9 (2026-08-19) already sketched a FIFO design under different names — `stock_lots` (with `unit_cost` and `quantity_received` *stored*, not derived) linked to the `stock_movements` row that created it (generic across any movement source), plus a separate `stock_movement_lot_allocations` table for the draw-down side. This session's `inventory_lots` is Purchasing-specific (FK to `purchase_order_receipts`, not `stock_movements`) and stores less (no `unit_cost`, no `quantity_received` — both derived/read through the relation instead). **These are two different designs from two different sessions, not yet reconciled.** Open question for next session: does `inventory_lots` replace Session 9's `stock_lots` sketch entirely (Purchasing is the only lot-creating source that exists or is imminent, so scope it narrowly), or does tying lots to `purchase_order_receipts` specifically become a problem once Production Output (finished goods) also needs to create lots — those would need a different cost formula (labor+material+overhead rollup, not a purchase unit price) and might not fit through the same FK. Not decided — flagged, not solved.

**Also not decided:** exact name/shape of the new orchestrating Action for "loop items + require ≥1 attachment + create the batch + create receipts + create lots, all in one transaction" — discussed as necessary but never named or designed step-by-step the way `ReceivePurchaseOrderAction`'s sequence was in Session 7. Whether it wraps/calls the existing `ReceivePurchaseOrderAction` per item, or supersedes it, is also open.

### 3 migrations written to disk (by the user, per standing preference) — 2 real bugs found in them
- `database/migrations/purchase/2026_09_12_111049_create_purchase_order_receipt_batches.php` — correct, matches the agreed design (`purchase_order_id`, `received_at`, `received_by`, `timestamps()`).
- `database/migrations/purchase/2026_09_12_111257_udpate_purchase_order_receipt.php` — **filename typo** (`udpate`), cosmetic only. Migration body itself is correct: drops `received_at`/`received_by` (+ their FK) off `purchase_order_receipts`, adds a nullable `purchase_order_receipt_batch_id` FK. `down()` correctly re-adds the dropped columns as nullable rather than `NOT NULL`, since by rollback time real rows may already lack values for either.
- `database/migrations/purchase/2026_09_12_111713_create_purchase_order_attachments.php` — **real bug, will fail at migrate time**: the FK column is named `purchase_order_batch_id`, but should be `purchase_order_receipt_batch_id` to match the actual parent table `purchase_order_receipt_batches`. `$table->foreignId('purchase_order_batch_id')->constrained()` — bare `constrained()` infers the referenced table from the column name by convention (strips `_id`, pluralizes), which resolves to `purchase_order_batches` — **a table that doesn't exist**. This needs the column renamed (and the migration file itself renamed to drop the "udpate"-style inconsistency isn't required, just the FK column name) before this can run. Everything else in this file (disk/path/original_filename/mime_type/size columns, `created_at`-only/no `updated_at`) matches the design correctly.
- **Not yet started**: `inventory_lots` (the FIFO-costing table from Session 11 — `product_id` denormalized, `purchase_order_receipt_id` FK unique, `remaining_quantity`) has no migration at all yet. Models (`PurchaseOrderReceiptBatch`, `PurchaseOrderReceiptAttachment`, `InventoryLot`) and the new orchestrating Action (loop items + require ≥1 attachment + create batch/receipts/lots in one transaction — still unnamed, see Session 11) are also still unwritten.

### Receipt condition dropdown added to the receiving UI
`pages/warehouse/inbound/purchasing/⚡detail.blade.php` — every non-fully-received item (desktop table row + mobile card) now has a `<flux:select>` populated from `PurchaseOrderItemReceiptCondition::cases()`, stacked directly under the "Receive Now" quantity input (grouped together since they're both facets of "what am I receiving right now for this item," not spread across a 7th table column). Still no `wire:model` — UI-only, same as the quantity input beside it. Since nothing binds a value yet, the unbound `<select>` defaults to showing whichever `PurchaseOrderItemReceiptCondition` case is listed first (`ReceivedAsExpected`) — worth deciding once this gets wired up whether that default-to-\"as expected\" behavior is actually wanted, since a worker could submit without ever touching a damaged-goods dropdown.

### Visual/interactivity pass — "apply the login page's formula" to the detail page, then a follow-up "too white" pass
Two separate rounds this session, both assistant-written directly to files per explicit "implement it" requests (same per-message-exception pattern as Session 11, not a change to the standing show-don't-write rule):

1. **Login-formula pass**: added the same `fade-slide-up` keyframe block used on the login page, staggered across the detail page's sections (PO Header → Summary → Items List → Attachments → Actions). Real interactivity added, not just decoration: every item row gets `x-data="{ qty: '' }"` bound via `x-model` to its quantity input — as a worker types a value, the row tints with an accent wash and (desktop only) an "Editing" chip fades in, giving real-time visual feedback on which items are actually part of the current submission. Attachment dropzone got an idle hover state plus a `scale-[1.01]` lift during active drag-over; the attachment file list now animates entries in/out via Alpine `x-transition` instead of snapping. Flux's own buttons (Cancel/Submit) were deliberately left untouched — fighting a design-system component's own hover/transition behavior was judged worse than leaving it alone.
2. **"Too white" diagnosis**: user felt the detail page was empty/flat. Root causes found and fixed, in order of leverage:
   - The Receiving Summary card was `max-w-md` alone inside a `max-w-6xl` container, leaving a large blank gutter beside it on any wide screen. Fixed by surfacing a second, previously-dead computation — `$itemsReceivedCount` was being calculated at the top of the file but never actually rendered anywhere — turned into a proper `itemStatusBreakdown()` computed method (received/partial/pending counts) and built a real **Item Breakdown** card (segmented progress bar + color+text legend) next to Summary in a `grid-cols-2` row. Deliberately not a fake KPI-tile trio — MASTER.md already rejected that pattern once on List PO; this uses only data that was already being computed and already real.
   - **Bigger root cause, confirmed by reading the actual layout files, not assumed**: the authenticated app's shared body background (`components/layouts/app/sidebar.blade.php` — traced via `<x-layouts.app>` → `layouts/app.blade.php` → this file — is the layout every authenticated page actually uses) was `bg-white dark:bg-zinc-800`, and every card is *also* white — literally white-on-white, distinguished only by a thin border. This contradicts MASTER.md's own documented `Background` token (`zinc-50` light / matching card-vs-background separation). Fixed at the shared-layout level (affects every authenticated page, not just this one) — first to `zinc-50`/`zinc-950` (matching MASTER exactly), then bumped further to `zinc-100` per the user's explicit follow-up ask for "a bit more grey." `components/layouts/app/header.blade.php` (confirmed via grep to be currently unreferenced/dead — nothing in `resources/views` points at it) got the same fix for consistency, in case it's ever revived.

The remaining card is now just the Supplier/Order Date/Expected Delivery/Created By info grid, no longer duplicating the title. Removed the now-genuinely-dead `$itemsReceivedCount` `@php()` line as part of this (superseded by `itemStatusBreakdown()`).
   - Added `shadow-sm shadow-zinc-900/5 dark:shadow-none` to every card on the detail page, per explicit user ask — **flagged directly to the user first** that this is in tension with MASTER.md's own documented rule ("prefer border-based separation over heavy shadows... don't regress to drop-shadow-heavy cards," a deliberate decision from Create PO's redesign). Kept deliberately subtle (same recipe the login card already used, borders kept, dark mode stays shadow-free) and **scoped to this page only** — MASTER.md itself was not edited, and no other Purchasing page got shadows added. If shadows should become the new app-wide standard, that's a separate decision still open.

## Session 13 (2026-09-12, same sitting as Session 12 continued) — Full backend chain for the receiving batch + FIFO costing built out: migrations, models, updated Action, new Service

Same session as 12 (no `/login` or date change in between, just a long continuous sitting that moved from UI polish into backend implementation). This is the payoff of Session 11/12's design work — everything discussed got built, reviewed iteratively (several real bugs caught and fixed live), and the costing table finally got its migration.

### Costing table renamed and scoped down (Option A, deliberately) — `raw_material_lots`
Picked up the FIFO discussion from Session 11 and pushed it further: confirmed (by reading the actual `ReceivePurchaseOrderAction` code, not assumed) that one `PurchaseOrderReceipt` produces exactly one `StockMovement` today, even though the relation is a `MorphMany` (schema allows more, current code just never creates more than one). Then the user raised a real gap the earlier design missed: `inventory_lots` (Session 11's name) implied it could represent *any* stock quantity, but its only FK (`purchase_order_receipt_id`) means it can only ever originate from a raw-material purchase — it has no way to represent WIP or finished-goods output from Production, even though `StockMovementType::ProductionOutput`/`ProductionInput` already exist anticipating exactly that.

**Decision (Option A, explicit, not defaulted into):** keep the table scoped to raw-material purchase receipts only, and build a separate mechanism for WIP/finished-goods lots later, if/when Production needs it — rather than generalizing the origin to a polymorphic relation now (Option B). Reasoning: going polymorphic now wouldn't just add a column, it'd break "unit cost is derived, never stored" — that currently works because it's one simple relation chain (`lot → receipt → item → unit_price`); a polymorphic origin means each origin type would need to supply cost differently (purchase price vs. a labor+material+overhead rollup), which is real complexity for a module (Production) that doesn't exist yet. Matches this project's own repeated "don't scaffold for a module that doesn't exist" principle (used before for the low-stock notification and for deferring FIFO draw-down itself).

**Renamed from `inventory_lots` → `raw_material_lot` → `raw_material_lots`** (final, plural) — deliberately avoiding two collisions: not "batch" (already reserved for `purchase_order_receipt_batches`, the delivery-visit concept — reusing it here would re-confuse two concepts that were deliberately separated earlier), and not "stock_lot" either (that was the *old*, different, superseded Session 9 sketch — generic origin via `stock_movements`, stored `unit_cost`/`quantity_received` directly — reusing that name would make old notes read as if they describe the same table when they don't).

**Final schema, iterated collaboratively over several rounds** (each round had a real bug caught before the next): `id`, `product_id` (denormalized, FK), `purchase_order_receipt_id` (FK, **unique** — enforces one lot per receipt at the DB level), `remaining_stock` (decimal(12,2) unsigned — no stored `unit_cost`, still derived live via the receipt→item→unit_price chain), `timestamps()` (both needed — `updated_at` because `remaining_stock` genuinely changes over time as production consumes it later; `created_at` because it's very likely what FIFO "oldest lot first" ends up sorting by, since a lot is created in the same transaction as its receipt). Migration written: `database/migrations/warehouse/2026_09_12_150307_create_raw_material_lot.php` (filename singular, table itself correctly plural — cosmetic only) — includes a composite index `(product_id, remaining_stock)` for the FIFO hot-path query.

**FIFO draw-down mechanics discussed** (guide-only, nothing built — Production doesn't exist yet so there's nothing to call this from): walk `raw_material_lots` for a product oldest-first, `remaining_stock > 0`, take `min(lot.remaining_stock, still_needed)` per lot, decrement, record the split in a not-yet-built `stock_movement_lot_allocations`-style table (needed because one consumption can legitimately span multiple lots at different costs), repeat until satisfied. Flagged two things for whenever this actually gets built: locking needs to extend to the lot rows during allocation (same concurrency reasoning `CreateStockMovementAction` already applies to the `Product` row), and there's an open question of whether `Product.current_stock` should stay an independently-maintained cache or be derived from summing lot balances (independent risks silent drift between the two numbers; derived removes the drift risk but changes how `CreateStockMovementAction` currently works). Neither decided — parked for when Production exists.

### Models — written/updated, each reviewed line-by-line, several real bugs caught and fixed live
- **`PurchaseOrderReceipt.php`** — went through 3 review rounds. Round 1 caught: `receivedBy()` still querying the now-dropped `received_by` column (would throw at runtime) — removed. Round 2 caught: new `purchaseOrderBatch()` relation named inconsistently with the class it points to (works, but confusing) — renamed to `purchaseOrderReceiptBatch()`; and a new bug *introduced* by that same edit — `purchase_order_item_id` got added to `#[Guarded(...)]` alongside the new batch FK, which would silently break `ReceivePurchaseOrderAction`'s existing mass-assignment `create([...])` call (DB would reject the resulting NULL on a NOT NULL column). Round 3: fixed to `#[Guarded(['id'])]` only, confirmed correct. Final state also has (added by the user independently, not prompted): `rawMaterialLot(): HasOne` → `RawMaterialLot` — correctly singular, matching the unique constraint.
- **`PurchaseOrderReceiptBatch.php`** — 2 rounds. Caught: `receivedBy()` missing its explicit `'received_by'` FK argument (default inference would've looked for `received_by_id`, which doesn't exist — exact same class of bug as round 1 above, just on a different model) — fixed. Caught: `purchaseOrderReceipt(): HasMany` named singular despite returning a collection — same exact issue this codebase already fixed once before for `Product::stockMovement()` → `stockMovements()` (Session 7) — renamed to plural. Final state has `purchaseOrder(): BelongsTo` and `purchaseOrderReceiptAttachments(): HasMany` both correct from the start.
- **`PurchaseOrderReceiptAttachment.php`** — **written by the assistant directly** (explicit request). Draft had a leftover `'received_at' => 'datetime'` cast copy-pasted from the batch model, even though this table has no such column — replaced with `const UPDATED_AT = null` (matches `StockMovement`'s immutable-ledger shape — an attachment is only ever created or deleted, never edited) + `'size' => 'integer'` + the `purchaseOrderReceiptBatch(): BelongsTo` relation.
- **`RawMaterialLot.php`** — reviewed once, correct as written: `#[Guarded(['id'])]`, `remaining_stock` cast to `decimal:2`, `product()`/`purchaseOrderReceipt()` both `BelongsTo` and both resolving correctly by Eloquent's naming convention.
- **Inverse relations added by the user independently** (not prompted, filled gaps the assistant had flagged as missing in earlier reviews): `Product::rawMaterialLots(): HasMany`, `PurchaseOrder::purchaseOrderReceiptBatches(): HasMany` — both verified correct against Eloquent's default FK convention.

### `ReceivePurchaseOrderAction` updated (by the user, guided — not written by the assistant)
Three changes, all confirmed correct on final review: removed `'received_at' => now()` from the `PurchaseOrderReceipt::create([...])` call (column no longer exists there — would have thrown a DB error), added `'purchase_order_receipt_batch_id' => $data->purchaseOrderReceiptBatchId`, and added a `RawMaterialLot::create([...])` call right after the receipt is created (`product_id`, `purchase_order_receipt_id` = the new receipt's id, `remaining_stock` = the quantity just received). `$data->receivedBy` still flows into `CreateStockMovementData`'s `createdBy` argument unchanged — confirmed this survives the schema change since it was never really "the receipt's `received_by`", it was always feeding the stock movement. `handle()` still returns only `$receipt`, deliberately — user confirmed not to bother returning/loading the lot from this Action.

### `ReceivePurchaseOrderData` DTO — reviewed, confirmed correct
Gained `?int $purchaseOrderReceiptBatchId` (nullable, matching the migration's nullable FK). `receivedBy` correctly kept even though the receipt no longer stores it — same reasoning as above (still needed for the stock movement).

### Action vs. Service decision — first thing to ever live in `app/Services/`
User asked directly whether the still-unbuilt "submit one whole receiving visit" orchestrator should be an Action or a Service. Answered against this project's own litmus test: `ReceivePurchaseOrderAction` calling `CreateStockMovementAction` doesn't make it a Service, because that call happens exactly once, as an intrinsic necessary consequence of one atomic event ("receiving an item" *means* the ledger updates). The new orchestrator calls `ReceivePurchaseOrderAction` a *variable* number of times (once per item actually submitted) on top of two other distinct concerns (batch creation, attachment storage) — that's a real multi-step workflow, not one atomic operation, hence Service.

**Built**, all reviewed/confirmed matching what was designed:
- `app/DTO/Purchasing/ReceivePurchaseOrderBatchData.php` — `purchaseOrderId`, `receivedBy`, `items` (plain array of `['purchaseOrderItemId', 'quantityReceived', 'receiptCondition']`), `attachments` (plain array of `['disk', 'path', 'originalFilename', 'mimeType', 'size']`) — plain arrays, not nested DTOs, matching how `CreatePurchaseOrderData->items` already does it.
- `app/Services/Purchasing/ReceivePurchaseOrderBatchService.php` — filters `items` down to only those with `quantity > 0` (via `bccomp`), throws if that leaves zero items or if `attachments` is empty (both checks run *before* any DB writes — cheap validation before the expensive part), creates the `PurchaseOrderReceiptBatch`, creates each `PurchaseOrderReceiptAttachment` row against it, then loops the filtered items calling `ReceivePurchaseOrderAction` once each with the batch id threaded through. All inside one `DB::transaction()` (nests fine with `ReceivePurchaseOrderAction`'s own transaction via savepoints, same pattern already used for `CreateStockMovementAction`). Deliberately **no PO-level lock/`ensureCanReceive()` check at the Service's own level** — `ReceivePurchaseOrderAction` already checks it on every call, and the "≥1 item" guard guarantees at least one such call always happens, so it's redundant to check twice. Flagged as worth revisiting if two workers submitting *different* batches against the *same* PO concurrently ever becomes a real concern (today only item-level locking exists, not PO-level).
- `app/Exceptions/PurchaseOrderReceiptBatchRequiresItemsException.php` + `PurchaseOrderReceiptBatchRequiresAttachmentException.php` — **written by the assistant directly** (explicit request), matching the existing exception shape exactly (`extends RuntimeException`, promoted readonly `?int $purchaseOrderId` for the technical/log message, translatable `userMessage()`). Both added to `lang/id.json`.

**⚠️ One small fix still outstanding, not yet applied**: the Service currently throws both new exceptions with no arguments (`new PurchaseOrderReceiptBatchRequiresItemsException()`), leaving `purchaseOrderId` null in the log message. Should be `new PurchaseOrderReceiptBatchRequiresItemsException($data->purchaseOrderId)` (and same for the attachment one) — flagged to the user, not yet fixed as of end of session.

### Bugs from Session 12 — status check (verified by re-reading the files, not assumed)
The `purchase_order_batch_id` → `purchase_order_receipt_batch_id` FK bug flagged in Session 12 **is now fixed** — confirmed via `grep` on `database/migrations/purchase/2026_09_12_111713_create_purchase_order_attachments.php`, column is correctly named now. The `udpate` filename typo is still there (cosmetic only, never mattered functionally). **None of these migrations have been run yet** (`php artisan migrate` not executed against any of this — batches, the receipts-update, attachments, or the new `raw_material_lots` migration).

### Frontend wiring — not started, this is the actual next step
Walked through the full shape needed (guide-only, nothing written): `⚡detail.blade.php`'s Livewire component needs `use Livewire\WithFileUploads`, a `public array $items` (keyed by item id, quantity + condition) bound via `wire:model` to the existing "Receive Now" inputs/condition selects (currently unbound — built UI-only, deliberately, back in Session 11), and `public array $attachments` replacing the current pure-Alpine file mock (files never reach the server today — the dropzone's visual drag-and-drop polish can stay, but the actual `<input type="file">` underneath needs `wire:model`, not just an Alpine `x-on:change` handler). A new `submit()` method needs to: authorize (`warehouse.receive`, already exists), validate (≥1 item, ≥1 attachment — the Livewire-side check, separate from the Service's own domain-level check), `->store()` each uploaded file to get its metadata (file storage happens in the component, not the Service — keeps the Service framework-agnostic, consistent with every other Action/Service in this codebase never touching Livewire-specific types), build `ReceivePurchaseOrderBatchData` from `$items` + the stored-file metadata, call `ReceivePurchaseOrderBatchService::handle()`, then handle success/failure. The Submit button itself needs `wire:click="submit"` (currently `type="button"`, inert) plus the same loading-state treatment already established on Create PO.

## Session 14 (2026-09-16) — Receiving flow finished, bug-fixed, and polished; Production module architecture brainstormed at length (guide-only)

New sitting, `main` branch, same uncommitted working tree as Session 13 (nothing has been committed across Sessions 12–14 yet — still flagged as a decision to make). Two unrelated threads: (1) finished what Session 13 left as "next step" — the receiving page is now a real, working, bug-fixed feature; (2) a long guide-only architecture discussion for the not-yet-started Production module, entirely user-driven brainstorming with the assistant pressure-testing/pushing back only when asked.

### Receiving flow — wired, tested in-browser, and fixed

**Fixed before wiring began:** the `PurchaseOrderNotReceivableExcepiton` typo (misspelled import in `PurchaseOrder.php`, would have fatally errored the first time `ensureCanReceive()` ever threw) — user fixed it themselves after the assistant traced the chain and found it. Confirmed via `git diff`: `PurchaseOrder.php` now correctly imports/throws `PurchaseOrderNotReceivableException`.

**`⚡detail.blade.php` fully wired** (assistant wrote code directly to this file across several explicit "implement it for me" requests this session — same per-message-exception pattern as Sessions 11–13, not a change to the standing show-don't-write rule):
- `use WithFileUploads`, `public array $items` (keyed by item id: `quantity` + `receipt_condition`, both start blank/empty-string deliberately — this is a fresh in-progress receiving event, not an edit of the historical `quantity_received` total; pre-filling from history would risk double-counting a delivery that hasn't physically happened), `public array $attachments`.
- `mount()` now actually calls `Gate::authorize('receive', $purchaseOrder)` — **this was a real, previously-unnoticed gap**: the page had zero authorization check before this session, reachable by any authenticated user regardless of role.
- Quantity/condition inputs bound via plain `wire:model` (deferred, no live network per keystroke, matching the app's established convention) directly into `$items`. The row-highlight/"Editing" visual feedback that used to run off a disconnected local Alpine variable now reads `$wire.items['{id}']?.quantity` directly — Livewire 3 exposes `$wire` to Alpine as a reactive proxy, so this updates instantly client-side even though the underlying `wire:model` is deferred (no network round trip needed for the visual feedback to work).
- File uploads: real `wire:model="attachments"` on the file input (replacing the old pure-Alpine mock that never reached the server), plus a drag-and-drop-to-real-input trick (`DataTransfer` + dispatched `change` event) so the existing drag/drop UI polish still actually feeds Livewire. Confirmed via Livewire's own JS source (`_finishUpload` in `WithFileUploads.php`) that multi-file uploads **append** to the existing array by default — no manual merge logic needed for the "upload twice" case.
- `submit()`: validates ≥1 item has quantity > 0, validates attachments (`required|array|min:1` + per-file `mimes:pdf,png,jpg,jpeg|max:10240`), validates each submitted item's `receipt_condition` via `Rule::enum(...)`, stores each attachment to the `local` disk, builds `ReceivePurchaseOrderBatchData`, calls `ReceivePurchaseOrderBatchService::handle()`, `Flux::toast()` + redirect on success.

**Three real bugs caught and fixed post-wiring, all found by actually testing in a browser (dev server booted via the `run` skill fallback pattern — no project-specific run skill existed, `php artisan serve` + `npm run dev`):**
1. **Missing Submit button wiring** — button was still `type="button"` with no `wire:click`, so submitting did nothing at all. Also missing: any success feedback (`Flux::toast` + redirect) after a successful submit.
2. **File-store ordering bug** — `submit()`'s attachment-mapping closure called `$file->store(...)` (which *moves* the temp upload off disk) **before** calling `$file->getMimeType()`/`getSize()` (which need to read the file's actual content), because PHP evaluates array-literal entries top-to-bottom in source order. Produced the exact error the user hit: `"Unable to retrieve the file_size for file at location: livewire-tmp/...png"`. Fixed by reordering the array so `path` (the `store()` call) is the last key set.
3. **Silent validation failures** — `addError()`/`validate()` were correctly detecting "no item has a quantity" and "no receipt condition selected," but nothing in the template ever rendered those errors (only `attachments` had an `@error` block) and nothing fired a toast on failure. Fixed: `Flux::toast(variant: 'danger', ...)` now fires on every failure path (wrapped the two `validate()` calls in try/catch to toast-then-rethrow), added `@error("items.{id}.receipt_condition")` under both desktop/mobile condition selects and `@error('items')` under the Items List heading, and gave the `receipt_condition.required` rule a custom message (Laravel's default was leaking the raw field path: *"The items.12.receipt condition field is required."*) — now reads *"Please select a receipt condition for this item."*

**Migration status double-checked, contradicts what `migrate:status` shows:** `php artisan migrate:status` only scans `database/migrations/` by default, so it can't see files under `database/migrations/purchase/` or `.../warehouse/` at all — makes it *look* like nothing ran. Verified the real state directly via `Schema::hasTable()`/`hasColumn()` in tinker instead: **all of it actually ran successfully** — `purchase_order_receipt_batches`, `purchase_order_receipt_attachments` (real table name, despite the migration filename saying `..._attachments` without `receipt` — cosmetic only), `raw_material_lots` all exist; `purchase_order_receipts.purchase_order_receipt_batch_id` exists and `received_at` is correctly gone. **Separately noticed via `git diff`:** someone (the user, outside this session) had added `$this->loadMigrationsFrom(database_path('migrations/purchase'))` to `AppServiceProvider::boot()` at some point and then **removed it again** — so item #22 from the old Next-Steps list (register the custom path) has effectively been tried and reverted; `--path=` is still required on every `artisan migrate` invocation targeting those folders.

Small cleanup fixed along the way: removed the "Sedang Diisi"/"Editing" chip from the item row's Status column (user found it superfluous) — the row's background-tint-while-typing visual feedback stays, only the text+icon chip is gone.

**New `lang/id.json` entries this session:** `"Select condition"`, `"Order Value"`, `"Please fix the highlighted fields before submitting."`, `"Please select a receipt condition for this item."` — all validated as well-formed JSON after each edit, same discipline as prior sessions.

### UI polish — List and Detail pages (assistant wrote directly, per explicit "implement"/"redesign" requests)

**`⚡list.blade.php` (warehouse PO Receipt list):**

Chosen specifically because it's less custom code than the old hand-rolled buttons, not more.
