First pass made every *row* its own individually-shadowed card (`border-separate` + `border-spacing-y-3`, rounded first/last `<td>` per row); user asked to scale that back to "just the table" as one card, not per-row — reverted to a single `rounded-xl shadow-lg` container around the whole table/list with plain `border-b` row dividers inside it. MASTER.md itself untouched; this is a page-scoped exception, not a system-wide change.

**`⚡detail.blade.php` (receiving detail):**
- Ran a `/ui-ux-pro-max` pass on the specific complaint that the header's floating "Total" felt disconnected from the rest of the page. Rather than inventing a new treatment, reused an already-established pattern from this exact codebase — the `bg-accent` block pinned to the bottom of a card, already used on View PO's Order Summary card and Create PO's Est. Total panel. Total moved out of the header entirely into a new third "Order Value" card in the Summary row (grid went `lg:grid-cols-2` → `lg:grid-cols-3`), with a small "Line Items" count as filler content in the card body (mirroring View PO's exact same card shape).
- Added the `border-s-[3px] border-s-accent` left accent border to the Item Breakdown card too, so all three summary cards match (previously only Receiving Summary had it).
- Removed the "Gudang · Penerimaan Pembelian" ("Warehouse · Purchase Order Receipt") eyebrow line from the header entirely, per direct request — header now starts straight at the PO number + status badge.

### Font experiment — Roboto (display) + Open Sans (body), applied directly per user request

Explicitly framed by the user as "I want to experiment" — asked first via `AskUserQuestion` whether to apply directly or just show the diff; user chose apply-directly. Swapped in `vite.config.js` (`bunny('Instrument Sans', ...)` → `bunny('Open Sans', ...)`, the self-hosted body font via Laravel's Vite font plugin) and `resources/css/app.css` (`--font-sans`/`--font-display` tokens), plus the CDN `<link>` that loads the display face (`space-grotesk` → `roboto`) in both `partials/head.blade.php` and `components/layouts/guest.blade.php` (Fira Code, the data/money font, untouched). **Explicitly still an experiment, not a finalized decision** — one-line revert each if it doesn't work out; `design-system/mims/MASTER.md` was not updated to reflect this (still documents Space Grotesk/Instrument Sans).

### Production module — architecture brainstorm (guide-only, nothing built; user explicitly building the schema themselves next)

Long back-and-forth, user leading with their own ideas each time and only asking the assistant for pushback/opinion at specific points (the standing "don't volunteer a recommendation unless asked" rule held throughout — assistant asked clarifying questions and reflected tradeoffs back rather than proposing solutions, until the user explicitly asked "what do you think" at two points). Nothing has been migrated, modeled, or coded — this is 100% design discussion to pick up from next session.

**Decisions reached, in the order they were reasoned through:**

1. **No fixed-step "production formula" with baked-in costs.** Original idea was a reusable formula defining ordered steps (Mixing → Cutting → Packaging) each carrying its own cost. Abandoned once the user realized raw material cost is FIFO-derived (varies every time, depending on which lots get drawn) — a formula can't pre-bake a cost that isn't knowable until actual consumption. Landed instead on: **everything (machine, worker, material, and therefore cost) gets decided fresh at the Work Order/Production Plan stage**, not defined in advance by a template.

2. **BOM is needed, but scoped to just material quantities, and is completely non-enforced.** A BOM exists per producible product (both WIP and Finished Goods need one, not just top-level finished goods) — defines *what materials, how much*, nothing about cost or sequence. Explicitly decided to be **just a template/pre-fill helper with zero enforcement** — a Work Order is free to deviate from its product's BOM entirely (different materials, different quantities). This means no validation code is needed to check "does this Work Order's material list match its BOM" — that check simply doesn't exist by design, not an oversight.

3. **`ProductType::Wip` already exists in the enum** (`'wip'` case, alongside `RawMaterial`/`FinishedGoods`) — added early, unused until now. Confirmed by reading the file, not assumed.

4. **WIP is a real consumable input, not just an inventory state.** A Work Order can consume a WIP item as material input the same way it consumes raw materials — meaning multi-stage chains are real (Plan A: raw materials → WIP; Plan B: that WIP → finished goods), not just a status label.

5. **One `WorkOrder` model, not several.** A single Work Order is scoped to **exactly one hop** — either raw materials → WIP, or WIP → finished goods — never the full raw-to-finished-goods chain in one record. Which hop a given instance represents is just data on that instance (a `hop_type`-style field, or derivable from the input/output product types), not a separate model/table per hop type. This also resolved an earlier concern the assistant raised about dropping "steps" losing shop-floor process visibility — steps aren't gone, they're promoted to be first-class Work Orders themselves, one per real production stage.

6. **Work Order output tracked as a lot, in one shared table — `work_order_output_lots`.** Every completed Work Order's output (WIP or finished goods) becomes its own trackable, cost-bearing, FIFO-drawable unit, same shape as `raw_material_lots`. Decided explicitly **against** splitting WIP-lots and Finished-Goods-lots into two tables: they're structurally identical (same origin — one `work_order_id` FK, same fields), the only difference is what `Product::type` the linked product happens to be — and this codebase already has a standing precedent for not splitting tables over `Product::type` (`products` itself is one table for all three types). Splitting would also mean duplicating the FIFO-draw-down query across two tables for zero real behavioral gain; the "don't let a Sale draw from a WIP lot" protection is just as reliable as a `whereHas('product', ...)` query scope on one shared table. Name chosen to describe the *origin* (what created it — a Work Order), mirroring how `purchase_order_receipt_batches` is named after its origin, not its contents — not `work_in_progress_product_lot` (the user's first instinct), since that name stops fitting once the table also holds finished-goods lots.

7. **Production lot cost must be computed once at completion and *stored*, unlike `raw_material_lots`' derive-live design.** `raw_material_lots` gets away with deriving cost on the fly because it's one cheap relation hop (`lot → receipt → item → unit_price`) to a value that's frozen once the PO leaves Draft. A Work Order's output cost is a rollup of a *variable-length list* of consumed input lots (raw material and/or WIP, possibly several) plus labor plus machine cost — recomputing that later would mean replaying the entire consumption history. So `work_order_output_lots` needs its own stored cost column (a cached/derived field, guarded from mass-assignment, same treatment as `products.current_stock`), computed once when the Work Order completes. A Finished-Goods lot's cost is therefore a further rollup **of WIP lot(s) already-computed cost** (plus that hop's own labor/machine) — the cost chain cascades through however many hops a chain of Work Orders has, each link using the previous link's already-stored number.

8. **A new "allocation" table is needed to record which lot(s) a Work Order's consumption drew from and how much** — necessary because one consumption event can legitimately span multiple differently-costed lots (FIFO splitting across lot boundaries), same reasoning already sketched (but never built) for the raw-material case back in Session 9. This table needs a **polymorphic `lot_type`/`lot_id`** (`morphTo`), reusing the exact idiom already established for `stock_movements.reference_type`/`reference_id` (a morph-map string key, not a raw FQCN) — because it points at two genuinely separate, differently-shaped, independently-existing tables (`raw_material_lots` and `work_order_output_lots`), unlike the WIP/FG-lot question above where the two things being unified were structurally identical. Two new morph-map entries will be needed in `AppServiceProvider::boot()` alongside the existing `'purchase_order_receipt'` one.
   - **Sequencing dependency, flagged explicitly:** this allocation table can't actually be built first in isolation — it needs an FK to *which Work Order's consumption* a row belongs to, so at minimum a skeleton `work_orders` table has to exist before or alongside it.
   - **Open, deliberately deferred by the user** ("let me try it myself first"): whether an allocation row references the Work Order directly (giving only "total material cost for this Work Order," no breakdown), or a more granular per-material-line identity within the Work Order (so cost can be broken down *by which material*, not just totaled) — matters if the future Cost Accounting module needs a materials breakdown, not just a total. **Not decided — pick this back up once the user has shaped the `work_orders` table.**

9. **Precision/rounding risk flagged, not yet resolved:** raw material inputs are measured in fractional KG (e.g., 1.36 kg of plastic) — multiplying `unit_price × quantity_drawn` for an allocation's cost is safe (no division involved), but computing a *new* lot's cost (rollup ÷ quantity produced) is a genuine division that can produce a non-terminating decimal, and this project's existing `decimal(12,2)` convention (fine for Rupiah amounts) may not carry enough precision for a value that gets multiplied forward again at the next hop — rounding error compounding across a multi-hop chain is exactly the failure mode `bcmath` discipline exists to prevent elsewhere in this codebase. **Possible direction floated, not committed to:** never store a divided *per-unit* cost at all — store each lot's *total* cost only, and compute whatever fractional proportion is needed fresh via `bcdiv`/`bcmul` at the moment of use, so rounding only ever happens once, at final display, never mid-chain. **Confirmed by the user:** production *output* quantities (termos body, lid, etc.) are always whole units/integers — the fractional-precision risk lives entirely on the raw-material *input* side, not the output-quantity side. Practical implication: `work_order_output_lots`' quantity/remaining-stock field can likely just be a plain integer column, unlike `raw_material_lots.remaining_stock` (`decimal(12,2)`).

**Nothing from this list has been migrated, modeled, or coded yet.** Next session should resume once the user has drafted the `work_orders` table shape themselves.

## Session 15 (2026-10-03/04) — ERD reference introduced; `inventory_lots` generalized; Create/List/View PO polish; project-wide Indonesian validation messages; "Penerimaan Barang" (Item Receipt) flow fully redesigned

New sitting, branch `main`. Two things changed the texture of this session versus earlier ones: (1) the user supplied `erd-lengkap.dbml` at project root — a 38-table reference ERD covering the *entire* planned system (Pengguna/Karyawan, Pembelian, Persediaan, Produksi, Penjualan, Akuntansi) — now the standing reference for new schema work; (2) the "show code, don't write it" rule (2026-08-26) was functionally suspended almost the whole session — the user granted direct-write permission explicitly ("yeah go ahead and write it") early on and kept extending it message-by-message for every subsequent UI/backend task, the same "per-message exception, not a rule change" pattern already noted in Sessions 11–14, just far more sustained this time. Worth revisiting explicitly next session whether this has quietly become the new default.

### ERD discrepancy audit (guide-only analysis, no code) — `erd-lengkap.dbml` vs. actual DB
Compared every already-built table against the new reference ERD (real migrations/enums read directly, not recalled from memory). Six real discrepancies surfaced, ranked by relevance:
1. **`raw_material_lots` vs. the ERD's generic `inventory_lots`** — the biggest one; see below, resolved this session.
2. **`stock_movements`** has no `inventory_lot_id`/`unit_cost`/`total_value` — the ERD bakes FIFO cost directly into every movement row, something Session 9 had already flagged as deferred/undecided. Still undecided — `stock_movements.amount` is still a bare quantity with zero cost info today.
3. **`purchase_order_receipts.receipt_condition`** — actual enum (`ReceivedAsExpected`/`More`/`Less`/`DamagedGoods`) describes *quantity-match* outcome; the ERD's (`good`/`damaged`/`incorrect_item`) describes *physical condition* and comes with an explicit rule ("only `good` creates a lot") that the real `ReceivePurchaseOrderAction` doesn't implement — it creates a lot unconditionally regardless of condition. Flagged, not fixed.
4. **`purchase_order_receipt_batches`** was missing a human-readable `receipt_number` (every other major document in this app has one) and modeled attachments differently than the ERD (child table vs. a single `attachment_path` column) — the attachment half got resolved this session (see below); `receipt_number` is still missing.
5. **`users`** — actual `UserRole` has 7 cases including `system_admin`; the ERD's 6-case enum has no admin/technical role at all, and the ERD's `employees`/`labor_rates`/`users.employee_id` trio doesn't exist yet (relevant once `work_order_labors` needs it).
6. Minor: actual money/quantity columns are `decimal(12,2)`; the ERD uses `decimal(18,2)`/`decimal(18,4)` throughout — not a correctness issue yet, just a convention gap if the ERD is the long-term target.

### `raw_material_lots` → `inventory_lots` — Option B generalization (explicit decision, not defaulted into)
User chose to reopen Session 13's "Option A" (narrow, derive-live cost) decision and go the other way: **one shared, polymorphic lot table for every origin** (purchase receipts now, Production receipts later), matching the ERD's `inventory_lots` shape — and explicitly folded Session 14's planned separate `work_order_output_lots` table into this same one too, rather than keeping two lot tables.

**Mechanics:** the old migration (`create_raw_material_lot.php`, still uncommitted) was rolled back and replaced in-place with a new one creating `inventory_lots` directly — clean since nothing had been committed yet. Schema, each column a deliberate choice (none defaulted):
```
id, lot_number (unique), product_id (FK, denormalized — same precedent as stock_movements.product_id),
source_type + source_id (morphTo, unique together — "one lot per source event", generically),
quantity_initial, quantity_remaining, unit_cost, value_remaining,
received_at, created_at, updated_at
indexes: (product_id, received_at, id) for the FIFO hot path
```
- **`unit_cost` is now *stored*, not derived** — forced by going generic: the old design worked because there was exactly one relation chain (`lot → receipt → item → unit_price`); once a lot can originate from a purchase receipt *or* a future production result, there's no single chain anymore, each origin has to compute and hand over its own cost. **Flagged as a real, unresolved tension**: this reopens the Session 14 Production-brainstorm precision concern (a production-origin lot's `unit_cost` is a genuine division, `total_cost / quantity_good`, which can produce non-terminating decimals that compound forward through multi-hop chains) — kept `decimal(12,2)` for consistency with the rest of the app's money columns, but this is exactly the kind of value Session 14's brainstorm worried about storing pre-divided.
- `quantity_initial`, `value_remaining`, `lot_number`, `received_at` as their own column (not reusing `created_at`) were all genuinely open questions the user resolved explicitly: store the initial quantity (self-contained, write-once, same denormalization precedent), add `value_remaining` now even though FIFO drawdown doesn't exist yet (shaping the table at its genesis rather than bolting it on later), give lots a human-readable number matching every other major document in the app, and keep `received_at` distinct from `created_at` since a production lot's "received" moment could someday differ from its row-creation moment.
- Morph map: reused the existing `'purchase_order_receipt' => PurchaseOrderReceipt::class` entry already in `AppServiceProvider::boot()` (same one `stock_movements` already shares) — no new entry needed yet; a `'production_receipt'` key (or similar) will be needed once Production actually creates lots.
- `lot_number` format mirrors `po_number` exactly: `Str::uuid()` placeholder on insert (since the column is unique + NOT NULL but the real value needs the row's own id), then `sprintf('LOT-%s-%06d', ...)` once the id exists — **one real bug caught and fixed**: first draft derived the year from `now()` (wall-clock), not from the lot's own `received_at` — inconsistent with how `po_number` derives its year from `order_date` (a domain/business date), not `now()`. Fixed to use `$lot->received_at->format('Y')`.
- **Model**: `RawMaterialLot` deleted, replaced by `InventoryLot` (`product(): BelongsTo`, `source(): MorphTo`). `PurchaseOrderReceipt::rawMaterialLot(): HasOne` → `inventoryLot(): MorphOne` (via the `source` morph). `Product::rawMaterialLots(): HasMany` → `inventoryLots(): HasMany`.
- **`ReceivePurchaseOrderAction`** updated to create an `InventoryLot` with `source_type = 'purchase_order_receipt'`, `source_id = $receipt->id`, `unit_cost` from the PO item's `unit_price`, `value_remaining` via `bcmul`. Verified end-to-end via tinker (`InventoryLot::create()` round-tripped correctly; cleaned up the test row).
- **Not done**: nothing yet writes a `source_type = 'production_receipt'` row — that's still Production-module work. `work_order_output_lots` from the Session 14 brainstorm needs an explicit "does this still exist as a separate table, or is it now just another `inventory_lots` source" decision next session — flagged above in the priority banner, not resolved here.

### Purchasing UI — Create/List/View PO all redesigned (assistant wrote directly throughout, per the sustained write-permission grant)
All three pages got the same treatment, applied incrementally across the session as each was requested:
- **Eyebrow text removed** from all three headers ("Pembelian · Draf Pesanan" / "· List Pesanan" / "· Order Detail") — user disliked the small all-caps label line above each title; removed outright, nothing replaces it.
- **Entrance motion**: the `fade-slide-up` keyframe convention (already established on login/receiving-detail pages in earlier sessions) copied into all three pages' own `<style>` blocks, staggered across sections (header → card(s) → table/list → summary).
- **Real interactivity, each tied to actual state** (not decorative): step-marker badges on Create PO turn into a green checkmark once that section is genuinely filled in (`isSupplierStepComplete()`/`isItemsStepComplete()`, two new plain methods); `wire:transition` on item rows/cards so add/remove/filter animates instead of snapping; the status badge and Total figure on View PO flash (`wire:key` tied to the value, so Livewire treats a changed value as a new node) when they actually change; press feedback (`active:scale-[...]`) added to essentially every button across all three pages; a focus-triggered row tint on Create PO's item rows via Alpine, no network round-trip needed.
- **Mobile card layout for Create PO's line items** — previously just `overflow-x-auto` on the same cramped table; added a `lg:hidden` stacked-card alternative (same idiom View PO/List PO already had) specifically because the price field was unreadably small below `lg`. Qty/Unit Price get their own grid cells instead of fighting for a `w-40`/`w-60` table column.
- **List PO table/card "stand out" pass** — went through a real back-and-forth worth recording: first pass collapsed the desktop table into the mobile card style entirely at the user's request ("use the card style for mobile and desktop"); user then clarified that wasn't the ask — **table stays for desktop**, cards for mobile, just both needed to visually "pop" off the page background. Reverted to the dual table+card layout and applied `rounded-xl bg-white dark:bg-zinc-900 shadow-lg shadow-zinc-900/10 dark:shadow-black/40` to both — reused verbatim from the one place in the app that had already made this exact flat-vs-shadow exception (the warehouse receiving list from Session 14), rather than inventing new shadow values. Same treatment then carried onto View PO's 3 info cards and item table/cards for consistency within that page.
- **Font consistency drive-by fix**: Create PO still used literal `font-mono` in several spots (never migrated when the rest of the app switched to the `--font-data` token in Session 9) — swapped to `font-data` throughout.
- **Date-field rules on Create PO**: `orderDate` is now `#[Locked]` (Livewire attribute — the actual server-side enforcement, confirmed by testing that a client-side `->set('orderDate', ...)` throws `CannotUpdateLockedPropertyException`) and the input disabled in markup too, so it's genuinely not user-editable, not just visually greyed out. `expectedDeliveryDate` briefly had its `after_or_equal:orderDate` rule removed on a misread of a user request ("make sure it can go before"), then explicitly reverted two turns later once the user clarified they actually wanted the opposite — the rule is back, **plus** a new defense-in-depth check: `CreatePurchaseOrderAction::handle()` now throws a new `InvalidDeliveryDateException` if `expectedDeliveryDate < orderDate` (checked before the transaction opens), caught in the Livewire component and shown via `Flux::toast(variant: 'danger', ...)`. A one-off DB audit during this found exactly one pre-existing bad row (`PO-2026-000008`, delivery date one day before order date) — **flagged, not fixed/deleted**, since the user only asked to check.
- **Navigation fixes**: Create PO's breadcrumb was missing the middle "Purchase Orders" hop (`Pembelian → Buat` instead of `Pembelian → Pesanan Pembelian → Buat`) — fixed to match View PO's already-correct 3-level pattern, plus a new `"Create": "Buat"` translation key. Sidebar's separate "Create Purchase Order" + "Purchase Order List" items collapsed into one ("Purchase Order", `:current` matching both routes) since having two entries pointing at what's really one flow read as redundant.
- **Sidebar now also surfaces Manager's and System Admin's own dashboards** — previously the sidebar loop only iterated `UserRole::operational()` (deliberately excluding Manager/SystemAdmin per the original Session 4 design, since they got the `view-dashboard` Gate override instead of their own nav group). Changed to `UserRole::cases()` (all 7 roles) — since the existing Gate already encodes exactly the right visibility rule (own role, or Manager/SystemAdmin's override), this one-line change correctly gives Manager a "Manager" group (→ `executive.dashboard`) and SystemAdmin a "System Admin" group (→ `admin.dashboard`), while a plain operational-role user still sees neither. Verified across all three role types via render tests.

### Project-wide Indonesian validation messages — `lang/id/validation.php` (new file, root cause found)
User noticed a validation error ("must be a date after or equal to order date") was still in English despite `APP_LOCALE=id`. Root cause, confirmed by checking the actual directory structure: `APP_LOCALE=id` only ever affected `__('literal string')` calls via `lang/id.json` (the project's established convention) — Laravel's *own* built-in validator messages (`required`, `after_or_equal`, etc.) come from a completely separate mechanism, `lang/{locale}/validation.php`, and that file never existed for `id` at all, so every rule-generated message silently fell back to the framework's bundled English default. This is a systemic, app-wide gap, not specific to one field.

**Fixed in two parts** (both needed — one without the other only half-works):
1. Created `lang/id/validation.php` — full Indonesian translation of Laravel's rule-message templates (mirrors the vendor English file's structure exactly, including the multi-type sub-arrays for `between`/`gt`/`min`/`max`/`size`/`password` etc.). Left the file's own `'attributes' => []` array empty deliberately — every component in this app already supplies its own labels via `validationAttributes()`, which takes precedence anyway; duplicating them in two places would just risk drift.
2. **`validationAttributes()` methods updated to return translated labels** (`__('Order Date')` etc., reusing the exact same `lang/id.json` keys already driving the visible UI labels) instead of hardcoded English strings — without this, the new Indonesian rule template would still wrap an English field name (e.g. "Kolom order date..."). Done for Create PO's `validationAttributes()` and added fresh `attributes` arrays to View PO's three previously-bare inline `$this->validate([...])` calls (`addItem`, `updateItem`, `reject`), which had no custom attribute labels at all before this. **Not done**: the warehouse Item Receipt pages' own inline `validate()` calls — same gap, not yet touched.

Verified via render tests throughout: a deliberately-bad date now renders *fully* in Indonesian ("Perkiraan Pengiriman harus berupa tanggal setelah atau sama dengan Tanggal Pesanan."), and View PO's reject-reason validation shows "Alasan Penolakan" instead of an auto-generated English-ish fallback.

### "Purchase Order Receipt" → "Item Receipt" — UI-text-only rename (scope deliberately narrowed via `AskUserQuestion`)
User asked to generalize "po receipt" → "item receipt" everywhere "to make it more general." Before touching anything, surveyed how deep the terminology actually goes — found it's not just display text: the `PurchaseOrderReceipt`/`PurchaseOrderReceiptBatch`/`PurchaseOrderReceiptAttachment` models, their tables, the `'purchase_order_receipt'` morph-map key (shared with the new `inventory_lots.source_type`), and the whole Action/Service/Exception chain all use the name. Given the very different blast radius between "rename some labels" and "rename the architecture," asked the user to pick explicitly — **chose UI-text only**. Changed: both warehouse pages' `#[Title(...)]` attributes, the list heading, and the matching `lang/id.json` key (also generalizing the Indonesian text itself, "Daftar Penerimaan Pembelian" → "Daftar Penerimaan Barang", matching a rename the user had already made to "Item Receipt" → "Penerimaan Barang" out-of-band before this). The underlying models/tables/morph-map/Actions were explicitly left alone — still named after "Purchase Order Receipt."

### "Penerimaan Barang" (Item Receipt) feature — full flow redesign, several rounds
By far the largest thread this session, iterated through several real direction changes as the user thought it through out loud:

**1. Generalized "Create Item Receipt" entry, source picker shell for Work Orders.** Original flow was: List page → click a specific PO row → land directly on that PO's receiving form. New flow: a dedicated "Create Item Receipt" entry point (sidebar / a header button) → a **Source** picker (Purchase Order vs. Work Order, Work Order visibly disabled with a "Coming soon" note — a deliberate shell, no WO code/schema touched, confirmed via `AskUserQuestion` that Work Orders don't exist yet and building them is explicitly out of scope for this task) → once a PO is picked from a searchable list, the page populates with that PO's items, editable, reviewable, then submitted — same backend as before (`ReceivePurchaseOrderBatchService`/`ReceivePurchaseOrderAction`, untouched). Mechanically: the old `⚡detail.blade.php` (route-bound to a required `{purchaseOrder}`) was renamed to `⚡create.blade.php`, its route param made optional (`{purchaseOrder?}`), and `mount()` branches on whether a PO was supplied — blank picker if not, straight to the populated form if so (either via the picker's redirect-after-select, or a direct link with the id already in the URL).
- **Custom listbox instead of a native `<select>`** for the PO picker, invoked via `/ui-ux-pro-max` — root-caused that Flux's free tier only ships a plain native-select wrapper (checked the actual vendor variants folder — only `default.blade.php` exists, no listbox/combobox), which is *why* a flat `"PO-2026-000007 — Supplier Name"` option string was hard to scan: a native `<option>` can't carry different fonts/weights per segment. Built a real Alpine-powered listbox (button + absolutely-positioned panel) where each row gives the PO number its own `font-data`/accent styling, the supplier name its own muted line below, and the status badge on the right — closes on outside-click/Escape, built from real `<button>` elements so it's keyboard-focusable with visible focus rings.
- **Folder/route/naming cleanup**: `resources/views/pages/warehouse/inbound/purchasing/` → `.../item-receipts/` (dropping "purchasing" from the path, since this is a generalized warehouse-receiving feature, not Purchasing-module-specific) — cascaded into the Livewire component-name strings, route names (`warehouse.inbound.item-receipts.list`/`.create`/`.detail`), and URIs. The actual `resources/views/pages/purchasing/orders/` folder (Create/List/View PO) was deliberately left alone — that one genuinely is the Purchasing module.

**2. Landing page flipped from List to Create, twice (user changed their mind mid-thread).** First asked for suggestions on "where should receipt status live if Create is the landing page" (answered as an open exploratory question, no implementation) — user initially said the current List-first flow was fine, then two messages later reversed and asked for Create-first after all. Implemented: sidebar's "Item Receipt" link now points straight at `.create`; the List page got demoted to a "View Receipt Status" ghost-button link from the Create page's blank state, rather than being deleted or merged in — kept as the lower-risk of the two options discussed.

**3. Split the editable Create flow from a new, genuinely read-only Detail view — eliminating a redundant second entry point.** User correctly flagged that once List rows linked into the same populated-and-editable `⚡create.blade.php` component used by the dedicated Create flow, there were now **two ways to reach the same editable receiving form** — unnecessary and confusing. Fix: built a brand-new `⚡detail.blade.php` (route `warehouse.inbound.item-receipts.detail`, `{purchaseOrder}` required, no optional/blank state) that reuses the same read-only computed stats (`receivingProgress`, `itemStatusBreakdown`, etc.) and the same info/summary cards and items table, but with the editable bits stripped entirely — no quantity inputs, no condition selects, no attachment upload, no submit button, just a "Back to List" action. List page row-clicks now go here instead of the create route. Route ordering (`list` → `create` → `detail`) was deliberately chosen and verified against Laravel's actual router (`Request::create()` + `Router::getRoutes()->match()`) so the literal `/create` segment never gets swallowed by the detail route's `{purchaseOrder}` catch-all. **Known, accepted tradeoff, flagged not fixed**: the detail page has no "Receive" shortcut back into the Create flow for that specific PO — if someone views a partially-received PO's status and wants to receive the rest, they go back to Create and re-pick it from the dropdown rather than one click through. Deliberate, to keep exactly one entry point into the editable form; revisit if this friction proves annoying in practice.

**4. Attachments collapsed from a child table to a single column, matching the ERD exactly.** Follow-up from the ERD audit above (`purchase_order_receipt_attachments` doesn't exist in the ERD at all — it models a single `attachment_path` directly on the batch). Asked directly "can the system exist without the separate table" — answered with the real tradeoff (you'd lose multi-file-per-visit support, which Session 11's original design explicitly anticipated) and the actual deciding question ("does one receiving visit ever legitimately need more than one photo") rather than picking a default; user confirmed one photo per visit is enough. Collapsed: new additive migration (the original `create_purchase_order_attachments`/`create_purchase_order_receipt_batches` migrations were already committed to git, so this couldn't be rewritten in place like the `inventory_lots` rename was — had to be a fresh migration adding `attachment_path` to the batch table and dropping the child table outright), `PurchaseOrderReceiptAttachment` model deleted, `ReceivePurchaseOrderBatchData`'s `attachments: array` → `attachmentPath: ?string`, `ReceivePurchaseOrderBatchService` simplified (no more per-file loop), and the UI's file input changed from `multiple` + a removable list to a single `wire:model="attachment"` with one "selected file" card. Verified end-to-end via a fake-file tinker test — submit succeeds, file lands on `purchase_order_receipt_batches.attachment_path`, and actually exists on disk; missing-attachment validation still correctly blocks submission.

### #26 — `receipt_condition` dropped entirely (domain decision, then built)
Worked through as a domain question, not a code fix. Findings, in order:
- The old enum mixed two dimensions (quantity-match vs. physical condition) and one-condition-per-receipt-row couldn't represent a partially-damaged delivery.
- **Client's actual process: receive everything, sort later** — nobody inspects at the dock, so the ERD's "only `good` creates a lot" rule assumes a process this client doesn't have. Unconditional lot creation is **correct**, not a bug — a deliberate, documentable deviation from the reference ERD (worth a sentence in the thesis).
- **Damaged goods are mostly still used in production**, with extra ingredients added to keep quality consistent. The cost impact therefore needs no special mechanism: per Session 14's non-enforced-BOM decision, the extra ingredient is just another material line on the Work Order, FIFO-costed like anything else, so the output lot's cost rises naturally.
- User concluded nobody downstream needs the condition → **dropped the column**.

Built (assistant-written, per request): migration `database/migrations/purchase/2026_10_05_100000_drop_receipt_condition_from_purchase_order_receipts_table.php` (run; `down()` re-adds as nullable), removed from `PurchaseOrderReceipt` cast, `ReceivePurchaseOrderData`, `ReceivePurchaseOrderAction`, `ReceivePurchaseOrderBatchService`, the Item Receipt `⚡create.blade.php` (dropdowns desktop+mobile, per-item validate loop, `Rule`/enum imports); deleted `app/Enums/PurchaseOrderItemReceiptCondition.php`; removed 8 dead `lang/id.json` keys. **`erd-lengkap.dbml` updated too**: `Enum receipt_condition` and the column removed, `quantity_received` got a note explaining always-create-lot + damaged-goods-into-production. Verified via a rolled-back service call (receipt + lot + stock movement created, no errors) and a grep (zero references left). Browser check of the form not done.

Backfill uses `DB::table()`, not the model, deliberately (migrations are frozen snapshots; models evolve). Year from `received_at`, same rule as the Session 15 `lot_number` fix. `receipt_number` added to `PurchaseOrderReceiptBatch`'s `#[Guarded]`; `ReceivePurchaseOrderBatchService` now does `new` + direct-assign UUID placeholder + `save()` + real number + `save()` (since a guarded field in `create([...])` would be silently dropped → NOT NULL failure). Only matters if the client ever needs gapless numbering.

## Session 17 (2026-10-05 → 2026-10-07) — Work Order feature complete (create · workers · overhead · release · print), employees & labor rates, overhead rates, custom pickers, many UI fixes

Same branch `main`, all uncommitted. Assistant wrote all code (2026-10-05 rule). Spans three days of one long conversation. Grouped by topic below, not chronologically.

### The production cost flow (explained to the user in plain terms — reuse this framing)
Every product's cost = **material + labor + overhead (BOP)**. One thermos, example numbers:
1. **Buy & receive** (✅): PP Merah received → `inventory_lots` row with its own cost (FIFO = oldest lot used first).
2. **Plan the WO** (✅): formula + workers + machine hours; rates and materials **copied** onto the WO (plan can't drift).
3. **Material Issue** (○ next): warehouse takes material from the oldest lots → real material cost.
4. **Production Result** (○): actual output / hours / machine hours → material + labor + overhead → **cost per unit**.
5. **Production Receipt** (○): warehouse receives output → new costed lot (WIP or FG).
6. **Assembly hop** (○): same stages again; Badan/Tutup lot costs carry forward into the thermos cost.
7. **Sell** (○): COGS from the oldest thermos lot.
Side track (○): overhead rate is an *estimate*; actual bills entered monthly; month-end variance closed by journal.

### Work Order — create, list, detail, release, print (✅ built)
- **Gates**: `production.view`, `production.create` (Production + Manager). **`WorkOrderPolicy`**: `viewAny`, `view`, `create`, `release` (create + Draft), `print` (view + not Draft/Cancelled).
- **`CreateWorkOrderAction`** (+ `CreateWorkOrderData` DTO): checks planned start ≥ today (`PlannedStartDateInPastException`), end ≥ start (`InvalidPlannedDateRangeException`), ≥1 worker (`WorkOrderRequiresLaborException` — `<<include>>` Mengalokasi Biaya Tenaga Kerja), formula active (`InactiveProductionFormulaException`), work center has an overhead rate today (`MissingOverheadRateException`); each worker active (`InactiveEmployeeException`) with a labor rate today (`MissingLaborRateException`). In one transaction: sets `status = Draft` **explicitly** (bug found: relying on the DB default left the returned model without a status, so policies saw null), derives `product_id` + `work_center_id` from the formula (never caller-supplied), copies `overhead_rate` and computes `planned_overhead_cost`, generates `wo_number` = `WO-{created_at year}-{id:06}` (UUID placeholder first, same pattern as PO/lot/RCV), **copies the formula items scaled to the target into `work_order_materials`**, and allocates labor rows copying each worker's current `hourly_rate`.
- **`ReleaseWorkOrderAction`**: re-reads the WO under `lockForUpdate()`, re-checks ≥1 worker (defense in depth — holds even for a WO created outside the form), `transitionTo(Released)`.
- **WO status flow** confirmed by the user: draft → released/cancelled; released → in_progress/cancelled; in_progress → completed/completed_short; terminal otherwise. **Decided: Released → In Progress fires automatically on the first Material Issue** (build it there; consider a `started_at` column).
- **Pages** (`resources/views/pages/production/work-orders/`): `⚡list` (status tabs, search, overdue chip per row; summary bar removed at user's request), `⚡create` (4 steps: Formula & Target — custom formula listbox grouped by work center; Schedule — start can't be in the past (picker `min` + `after_or_equal:today` + Action check); Workers — `<x-picker>` per row, hours, live cost; Machine & Overhead — equation panel *work center & rate × machine hours = planned overhead*; plus a live Material Requirements preview from the formula with shortage chips — warn at planning, enforce at issue), `⚡show` (product/schedule/output cards, workers table with planned labor cost, overhead strip, planned materials read from **`work_order_materials`** not the live formula, Release modal, Cetak button, "Terakhir dicetak").
- **Printing** (`<<extend>> Mencetak Perintah Kerja`; floor staff don't use the system — they work from paper): browser print page chosen over dompdf. `GET /production/work-orders/{wo}/print` → invokable `App\Http\Controllers\Production\PrintWorkOrderController` (authorizes `print`, sets `printed_at` = last print) → standalone `resources/views/production/work-orders/print.blade.php` (own CSS, A4, auto print dialog). Sheet: plan header, materials with blank Issued/Used + 2 spare lines, workers with blank Actual Hours/Signature, blank Production Log (date/shift/good/reject/machine hours/notes/signature — **the paper is the input form for Production Results later**), one signature box **Kepala Produksi**. **No Rupiah amounts on the sheet.** Open question for later: who types the paper log into the system (submitted_by / verified_by in the ERD).
- Dev data: `ProductionSeeder` (WIP-001 Badan Termos, WIP-002 Tutup Termos, FG-001 Termos 1L Merah; formulas F-WIP-001/002 on Molding, F-FG-001 on Assembly; overhead rates Molding Rp 15.000/h, Assembly Rp 40.000/h). Test accounts: `production@test.com`, `accounting@test.com` (password `password`).

### Employees & labor rates (✅ built)
- Use cases: **Manajemen — Mengelola Data Karyawan**, **Akuntansi — Mengelola Biaya Tenaga Kerja**. Gates `employees.manage` (Manager), `labor-rates.manage` (Accounting, Manager); `EmployeePolicy`, `LaborRatePolicy`.
- `employees`: `employee_code` (`EMP-000001`, generated, no year — master record not a dated document), `name`, `hire_date`, `status` (active/inactive — **deactivate, never delete**). **`department` and `position` removed at the user's request** (workers are general laborers — operators/assemblers, no distinction), via editing the uncommitted migration + rollback/re-run. ERD updated to match. `users.employee_id` deliberately **not** built ("leave it for now"; explained: links a login account to an employee record — useful later for attribution/offboarding).
- `labor_rates` effective-dated: `SetLaborRateAction` never overwrites — locks the employee row, rejects a start ≤ the latest rate's start, closes the open rate the day before the new one. `Employee::laborRateOn($date)`.
- `work_order_labors`: `planned_hours`, **copied** `hourly_rate`, `planned_cost`; unique (work_order, employee).
- Pages: `/management/employees` (list, Active/Inactive tabs, create/edit modal), `/accounting/labor-rates` (current + upcoming rate, Set Rate modal, History modal). Sidebar entries under Manager / Accounting. `EmployeeSeeder` (5 workers, Rp 22–26k/h).

### Overhead (BOP) design and build (✅ rates + applied overhead built; actual + closing designed only)
- Facts from the user: 9 identical molding machines + 1 assembly line; Akuntansi owns overhead; depreciation typed in manually monthly (no `machines` table); one electricity meter (can't split by work center); variance wanted.
- **Cost driver = machine hours, typed in manually for every work center.** On the assembly line 4 workers × 8h = 32 labor hours but **8 machine hours** (one line); on molding each worker runs their own machine, so it usually equals labor hours. Rate set by Akuntansi = (estimated electricity + depreciation + other) ÷ budgeted machine hours, per work center — one combined estimate; actual overhead and variance are **factory-wide** (one meter).
- Built (additive migrations `production/2026_10_07_100000…100300`): `work_centers` (MOLDING, ASSEMBLY inserted **by the migration** — reference data the schema needs), effective-dated `overhead_rates`, `production_formulas.work_center_id` (backfilled; ERD's `overhead_rate_per_unit` **dropped**), `work_orders.planned_machine_hours` / `overhead_rate` (copy) / `planned_overhead_cost`. `SetOverheadRateAction` (locks work center row), `WorkCenter::overheadRateOn()`. `InvalidLaborRateEffectiveDateException` generalized → **`InvalidRateEffectiveDateException`** (shared). Gate `overhead.manage`, `OverheadRatePolicy`, page `/accounting/overhead-rates` (card per work center, Set Rate, History).
- Designed in the ERD, **not built** (need Production Results first): `overhead_expenses` (period, category electricity/depreciation/other, amount — factory-wide), `overhead_period_closings` (period unique, applied/actual/variance, journal link), `overhead_variance` account. Open: lock a month once closed?; closing journal needs the Accounting module; variance disposal (all to COGS/P&L vs. prorated). Also raised, not decided: `overhead_expenses.work_center_id` (optional, depreciation *could* be split), and the ERD lacks account keys for actual overhead / accumulated depreciation.

### Work Order plan fully copied at creation (✅)
`work_orders.work_center_id` (FK, copied from the formula) + **`work_order_materials`** (`work_order_id`, `product_id`, `quantity_planned`; unique pair; no timestamps) via migration `production/2026_10_07_110000_copy_formula_plan_onto_work_orders.php`. Verified: editing a formula after WO creation leaves the WO's work center and materials unchanged. Everything a WO was approved with now lives on the WO (product, work center, materials, labor rates, overhead rate); the formula is only a template used once. Plan vs. actual will compare per `product_id` against `material_issue_items` / `production_result_materials`; extra material (damaged-goods compensation) shows as unplanned. ERD note on `production_result_materials.quantity` updated: pre-filled from `work_order_materials`, proportional to the result's output.

### UI components & fixes (✅)
- **`<x-picker>`** (`resources/views/components/picker.blade.php`) — generic searchable listbox replacing native `<select>`s: options `['id','title','subtitle','meta','disabled_reason']`, `size` `sm` (h-8) / `md` (h-10, matching Flux inputs), search box, ↑/↓ within the panel, Esc, `aria-selected`, red border on error, unavailable options show *why* ("Sudah dipilih", "belum ada tarif", "Belum ada produk"). Trigger shows **only the name** once chosen. Used for: WO workers (`workerOptions($index)`), PO products (`productOptions($index)` with catalog price/unit), PO supplier (`supplierOptions`, product count). The formula dropdown on Create WO is its own listbox (grouped by work center, type chips). `worker-picker.blade.php` was created then replaced by the generic one.
- **Picker positioning saga (lessons):** native select can't be styled → custom listbox. Inside the PO `overflow-x-auto` table it was clipped → tried `x-anchor.fixed` (misplaced: transformed ancestors break `position: fixed`) → tried `x-teleport="body"` (**broken with Livewire**: every re-render re-syncs the teleported copy from server HTML and wipes Alpine's inline `display:none`/position styles → orphan panels in the body grid) → **final: plain `absolute` panel inside the component** (opens upward if no room below), which works because the rows are now grids with no clipping container, plus the pages' `fade-slide-up` animation changed `both` → `backwards` so no transform (stacking context) lingers after it ends.
- **PO line items (Create PO)**: table → grid; then **container query** (`@container` + `@3xl:`, Tailwind 4.3.2): when the form column is narrow (~550px on the user's screen) each item is two lines (product full width + remove; then Qty / Unit Price / Subtotal with mini labels), wide = one row under headers. Qty unit ("Kg" + stock dot) is an input suffix; price has an "Rp" prefix. Product code under the input removed.
- **WO workers**: grid with `minmax(0,1fr)` instead of a squeezed table; rate shown under cost.
- **`<flux:error>` ignores its slot** — only renders the `message`/`name` prop. All 28 `@error … <flux:error>{{ $message }}</flux:error>` usages (Create/View PO, Create WO, rate pages) were silently empty; fixed to `<flux:error :message="$message" />`.
- **Field rows jumping on error**: grid rows default to `align-items: stretch`; added `items-start` to form-field grids (Create WO, Employees modal, Create/View PO).
- Formula/supplier fields use `flux:field` + `flux:label` so labels align with neighbouring Flux inputs; triggers are exactly Flux's input height.
- Date rules: WO planned start ≥ today; PO expected delivery picker `min` = order date (server rule already existed).
- Translations: many new `lang/id.json` keys (incl. product type labels and WO status labels).

### Known quirks / caveats recorded this session
- **Blaze** (Flux's compiler) can emit `Undefined array key … Unblaze.php:87` only while compiling a just-edited view — in a browser, one error page on the first load after an edit, gone on refresh. Vendor issue.
- Running `php artisan view:clear` while the dev server serves a request can cause a one-off `filemtime(): stat failed` (race). Not needed in dev — Blade recompiles on mtime change. Assistant stopped running it.
- `migrate:rollback` **needs `--path`** for subfolder migrations (otherwise "Migration not found", nothing happens), and **`--step` counts migrations, not batches**.
- Test runs consumed auto-increment ids (WO numbers up to ~000019, PO-2026-000010) — gaps are normal.
- Timezone (#19) now matters for "today" rules (WO start, PO delivery): between 00:00–07:00 WIB the server's UTC "today" is still yesterday.

Same branch `main`. Started with commit `beba6f5` "production basic" (Sessions 16–17). Everything below is uncommitted. Assistant wrote all code (2026-10-05 rule). Full build details are in Next Steps 0b–0d and the top-of-file Dev environment note; this is the index.

- **`stock_movements` FIFO columns** (0b): `amount` → `quantity`, `inventory_lot_id`, `unit_cost`, `total_value`; the two missing lots from PO-2026-000004 recreated.
- **Material Issue built and verified** (0c): rules + flow decided first (partial issues, unplanned lines with a note, over-issue allowed, Draft → Post, no pick list, short stock blocks the whole issue).
- **Morph map tidied**: `MaterialIssueItem` imported with `use` in `AppServiceProvider` like `PurchaseOrderReceipt`; key `material_issue_item` verified to resolve.
- **Production Results + Material Return designed** (0d): one result per WO; material used = reported actual; consumption FIFO by issue line; leftovers returned by a separate Gudang document into the original lots, last drawn first; WO material cost = issued − returned (not consumed × average cost). Three ERD conflicts found, then the ERD was updated to match (45 tables, see 0d).

## Session 19 (2026-10-09) — Production Results design finished; warehouse pages hide costs

Same branch `main`, uncommitted (on top of Session 18).

- **Production Results / Material Return design completed** (all decisions in Next Steps 0d, ERD updated): Kepala Produksi enters the result with no approval (Draft → Post); one reject type, cost absorbed by good units; no WO status for returns; return shortfall/surplus recorded at the actual amount and journaled as material variance (`material_variance`), not WO cost. User reminder logged: Akuntansi needs COA management (Next Steps #35).
- **Warehouse staff no longer see money (need-to-know).** New gate **`costs.view`** (Accounting, Manager) in `AppServiceProvider`. Hidden from users without it:
  - Material Issue list: Cost column and the mobile cost text; empty-row `colspan` adjusts.
  - Material Issue detail: Total cost cell (grid 4 → 3 columns) and the per-line cost and unit cost. The FIFO trail stays but shows lot + quantity only.
  - Item Receipt list: PO Total column and the mobile total.
  - Item Receipt create and detail: the "Order Value" card (grid 3 → 2 columns; the item count is still in the receiving summary).
  Verified by rendering every page as warehouse (0 "Rp") and manager (amounts shown). Visual layout not verified; check in the browser.
- **Material Issue list: "Keluarkan bahan" disabled once every planned material is fully issued** (user request, 2026-10-09), with a tooltip ("Semua bahan sesuai rencana sudah dikeluarkan.", 1 translation key added). UI only; the create route itself is not blocked. An open draft still shows "Buka draf". Open: this hides the path for extra or unplanned issues on a fully issued WO (allowed by the Session 18 rules), and fully issued WOs still appear in the "needs materials" tab and its count.
- **Enum labels translated at the source.** Some statuses showed in English (WO list and detail, every PO status, the sidebar role heading) because `->label()` was echoed without `__()` in about 15 places. Fix: every enum `label()` (`DocumentStatus`, `EmployeeStatus`, `ProductType`, `PurchaseOrderStatus`, `UserRole`, `WorkOrderStatus`) now returns `__(match …)`, so callers never need to wrap it; the existing `__($x->label())` calls are harmless (an Indonesian string has no key, so it comes back unchanged). Added 9 keys: PO statuses (Menunggu Persetujuan, Disetujui, Ditolak, Diterima Sebagian, Diterima Lengkap, Ditutup) and roles (Penjualan, Manajemen, Admin Sistem); `lang/id.json` now 409 keys.
- **Production Results built** (Next Steps 0d has the full build note): one result per WO, Kepala Produksi records and posts; WO → Completed; `completed_short` and WO `closed_*` removed; costs fixed on posting with the leftover priced at the last-drawn lots.
- **Missing translations swept app-wide** (user spotted "No purchase orders found." on Daftar Penerimaan Barang): a scan of every `__()` key in `resources/views` + `app` against `lang/id.json` found 9 missing on business pages (item receipts empty state / validation / toast / "Submitting...", PO list "Create Purchase Order", PO create "Loading supplier details...", header "Platform"/"Search", welcome) — added (467 keys). **Not translated yet: ~62 starter-kit keys** on settings/auth pages (2FA, passkeys, password update, delete account). Scanner kept in the scratchpad pattern: regex over `__('…')`/`trans_choice('…')`.
- **Draft WOs editable + rates refreshed at release + shortage warning at release** (decided 2026-10-09: "allow edit, refresh rates at release, B"). Shared plan logic moved into trait `App\Actions\Production\Concerns\BuildsWorkOrderPlan` (`validatePlan()` = start ≥ today, end ≥ start, ≥1 worker, active formula, work-center overhead rate today; `copyPlan()` = overhead rate + planned overhead, recipe scaled to target into `work_order_materials`, workers with today's rates into `work_order_labors`, replacing the old copy). `CreateWorkOrderAction` uses it; new **`UpdateWorkOrderAction`** (locks the WO, `WorkOrderNotEditableException` unless Draft, refills formula/product/work center/target/dates/machine hours, rebuilds the copy). `WorkOrderPolicy::update` (production.create + Draft). Route `production.work-orders.edit` → the **create page doubles as the edit page** (`mount(?WorkOrder)` prefills; `#[Locked] workOrderId`; heading "Ubah Perintah Kerja"; save calls Update or Create). **`ReleaseWorkOrderAction` now re-copies rates on the release day**: each worker must still be active and have a rate today (`InactiveEmployeeException` / `MissingLaborRateException`), `hourly_rate` + `planned_cost` updated; work center overhead rate today (`MissingOverheadRateException`) → `overhead_rate` + `planned_overhead_cost`. Rates are frozen from release on. **Release dialog lists materials whose stock < planned** ("Stok tidak cukup untuk n bahan", need vs stock) but still allows release (option B); stock isn't reserved, so it's a snapshot and Material Issue posting stays the hard check. WO detail has an Edit button for drafts. Verified (rolled back): create → edit target 100→250 rescales materials 13.6/0.5 → 34/1.25, hours and overhead updated; stale rates (set to Rp 1) restored to today's on release; released WO edit → 403. 9 translation keys (476). ERD notes on `work_orders.overhead_rate` / `work_order_labors.hourly_rate` updated. Page `#[Title]` still says "Create Work Order" in edit mode (browser tab only).
- **PP Hitam stock mismatch found + test data made consistent (2026-10-09).** User saw "diminta 18.00, tersedia 15.00" posting MI-2026-000007 (20 kg) with 17 in stock. Cause: `products.current_stock` had been set to 17 directly in the DB while lots/ledger said 102 (LOT-007: 2 + LOT-016: 100 from a receipt at 08:04). The posting check uses **lots** (102 ≥ 20, passed); FIFO drew 2 from LOT-007 (current_stock 17→15), then the `CreateStockMovementAction` safety net refused the 18 kg draw from LOT-016 (15 < 18), rolled back. User wanted a short-stock test case, so a **one-off dev fix** recorded an OUT `stock_adjustment` movement of 85 kg from LOT-016 (no reference, written directly so current_stock isn't subtracted twice): current_stock = lots = ledger = **17**. Posting MI-2026-000007 now fails correctly with "diminta 20.00, tersedia 17.00". **Offered, not done:** make the posting check also lock + compare `current_stock` and report line totals, with a clear message if the total and the lots disagree; a real Stock Adjustment feature (ERD `adjustment`) is the proper way to record lost/damaged stock. Note: editing `current_stock` by hand always breaks the invariant — adjust through movements.
- **Document number prefixes (user asked what RES means, kept as is 2026-10-09):** PO = Purchase Order, RCV = receipt batch, LOT = inventory lot, WO = Work Order, MI = Material Issue, **RES = production RESult** (Hasil Produksi). Alternatives discussed, not chosen: PR (clashes with Purchase Requisition / the future Production Receipt), PRS, HP (Indonesian, breaks the English pattern). Format everywhere: `{PREFIX}-{year}-{id:06}`, generated in the save/create Action after the first insert. Added since: **MR** = Material Return (Retur Bahan), **PRC** = Production ReCeipt (Penerimaan Produksi) — PR avoided (clashes with Purchase Requisition, too close to RES). User asked what PRC means (2026-10-10), explained, kept as is.
- **Produksi pages keep showing costs** (decided 2026-10-09): the Kepala Produksi needs them. WO create and detail show labor rates and planned labor and overhead cost to Production users, unchanged. `costs.view` is only for pages where the role works with quantities only (warehouse).

## Session 20 (2026-10-10) — Material Return built

Same branch `main`, uncommitted (on top of Sessions 18–19). Assistant wrote all code (2026-10-05 rule). Built from the design settled 2026-10-08/09 (Next Steps 0d). Choices made while building (user can change): number prefix **`MR`**; **one return per production result** (a cancelled draft frees the slot).

- **Shared walk:** `MaterialIssueItem::reverseDraws($qty)` — the line's OUT movements newest first, each part = movement, quantity, value (whole draw = its `total_value`, partial = qty × unit cost). `leftoverValue()` now sums it. Used by the production result (leftover pricing) and the return (which lots to refill) — same rule, one place.
- Migration `warehouse/2026_10_10_100000_create_material_returns_tables.php`: `material_returns` (`return_number` MR-{year}-{id}, `work_order_id`, `production_result_id`, `status` document_status, `created_by`, `received_by`/`received_at` set on posting) + `material_return_items` (per **issue line**: `material_issue_item_id`, `product_id`, `quantity_expected` = issued − consumed, `quantity` = actually received, `total_cost` = Σ IN movements, `variance_value` = expected value − returned value, + shortfall / − surplus; unique return+line). Matches the ERD.
- `StockMovementType::MaterialReturn` (`material_return`); morph key **`material_return_item`**; gate **`warehouse.return`** (Warehouse, Manager); `MaterialReturnPolicy` (update/post/cancel = draft); `ProductionResult::materialReturns()`.
- Actions in **`App\Actions\Warehouse`** (new namespace): `SaveMaterialReturnDraftAction` (static `checkReturnable()` = result posted, no other non-cancelled return, something left over, received ≤ ever issued per product; static `allocate()` = per product over the WO's issue lines **newest issue first**, each up to the line's quantity, so the leftover tail is filled first and a surplus reaches further back; a row for every line with an expected leftover even if 0 came back), **`PostMaterialReturnAction`** (locks the return, the issue lines and every lot involved in id order; re-allocates; for each part refills the lot `quantity_remaining`/`value_remaining` and writes an IN movement via `CreateStockMovementAction` with the draw's unit cost and exact value; sets `total_cost` + `variance_value`; posts), `CancelMaterialReturnAction` (draft only). Exceptions `InvalidMaterialReturnException` (RESULT_NOT_POSTED, NOTHING_TO_RETURN, ALREADY_RETURNED, MORE_THAN_ISSUED), `MaterialReturnNotEditableException`. No journal yet (variance stored for the Accounting module).
- Pages `resources/views/pages/warehouse/inbound/material-returns/`: `⚡list` (tab 1 "Sisa bahan menunggu retur": posted results with leftover and no posted return, leftover per product, "Terima retur" / "Buka draf"; tab 2 return documents, variance column only with `costs.view`), `⚡form` (create `/create/{productionResult}`, redirects to an open draft; edit; per product issued / used / expected back / **received** (pre-filled with expected) / difference), `⚡show` (expected vs received per line, lot trail "Dikembalikan ke (lot terakhir diambil dulu)", rupiah + material variance only with `costs.view`; Post / Cancel modals). Sidebar Gudang → "Retur Bahan". Production result detail now shows the return number/status (or "menunggu gudang") as text. 38 translation keys (514).
- **Verified (rolled back) on WO-2026-000020** (PP Hitam issued 15 + 5 kg, used 18): leftover sits on the newer issue line (#19); 1.5 kg received went back into **LOT-2026-000017** (the last-drawn lot, 28.5 of 30 kg); variance Rp 13.000; stock = lots = ledger; WO material 468.000 + returned 39.000 + variance 13.000 = 520.000 issued; second return and over-return refused; warehouse sees no rupiah, manager sees the variance; leftover tab clears after posting; production user gets 403 on warehouse pages. Visual layout not verified — check in the browser.
- **Result page return banner (user request):** removed the sentence "Sisa bahan dikembalikan ke gudang melalui retur bahan. Biayanya tidak dibebankan ke perintah kerja ini."; once the result is posted, the materials section shows a **callout**: green "Semua bahan yang dikeluarkan terpakai — tidak perlu retur bahan." when there's no leftover; amber ⚠ "Retur bahan: menunggu gudang" (or "draf di gudang (MR-…)") + "Harus dikembalikan: 2 Kg PP Hitam"; green "Sisa bahan sudah dikembalikan ke gudang" + MR number/time once the return is posted. All four states render-tested. 4 keys added, the removed sentence's key deleted (517).
- **Production Receipt built (Stage 5).** Decisions 2026-10-10: the receipt starts **automatically** (a posted result appears on Gudang's list); the user first chose several handovers, then changed to **ONE handover per result, like Material Return** (Draft → Post); **no surplus** (received ≤ good); lot priced at **the result's unit cost** and units counted short are a **loss** (option A — keeps the product's cost honest; B would hide the loss in a higher unit cost). Choices made while building: prefix **`PRC`**; received must be > 0.
  - Migration `production/2026_10_10_110000_create_production_receipts_table.php`: `production_receipts` (`receipt_number`, `production_result_id`, `product_id`, `quantity_expected` = good, `quantity` = counted, `unit_cost`/`total_value`/`shortage_value` set on posting, `notes`, `status` document_status, `created_by`, `received_by`/`received_at`). ERD table rewritten to match (old handover/discrepancy design and its two enums `discrepancy_resolution`, `production_receipt_status` removed).
  - Model `ProductionReceipt` (`lot()` morphOne on `inventory_lots.source`, `shortageQuantity()`, `transitionTo`, `ensureIsDraft`); `ProductionResult::productionReceipts()`; morph key **`production_receipt`** (lot source + movement reference). Policy `ProductionReceiptPolicy` on the existing gate **`warehouse.receive`**.
  - Actions in `App\Actions\Warehouse`: `SaveProductionReceiptDraftAction` (static `checkReceivable()`: result posted, one live receipt per result, 0 < qty ≤ good), **`PostProductionReceiptAction`** (locks receipt + result, re-checks; creates the lot `LOT-{year}-{id}` with `source_type` production_receipt at the result's unit cost — **if everything arrived the lot takes the result's exact `total_cost`**, otherwise qty × unit cost; IN movement type `ProductionOutput` via `CreateStockMovementAction`; `shortage_value` = total_cost − lot value), `CancelProductionReceiptAction`. Exceptions `InvalidProductionReceiptException`, `ProductionReceiptNotEditableException`. No journal yet.
  - Pages `resources/views/pages/warehouse/inbound/production-receipts/` (`⚡list` waiting / documents, `⚡form` reported good vs received (pre-filled) + difference + notes, `⚡show` quantities, lot created, unit cost / lot value / shortage only with `costs.view`, Post modal warns about a shortage). Sidebar Gudang → "Penerimaan Produksi". The production result page shows an output banner (amber waiting/draft, green received with lot number, amber with the shortage line). 38 translation keys (555).
  - **Verified (rolled back) on RES-2026-000006** (295 Badan Termos, total Rp 1.938.000, unit 6.569,49): all 295 → lot value exactly 1.938.000; 290 → lot 1.905.152,10 + shortage 32.847,90 = 1.938.000; stock = lots = ledger; 296 / 0 / second receipt refused; warehouse sees no rupiah, manager sees the shortage; waiting list clears; production user 403. Visual layout not verified.
- **Renamed in the UI (user request, 2026-10-10): "Penerimaan Hasil Produksi" → "Penerimaan Produksi"** (sidebar, heading, breadcrumb, edit title, messages; the result-page banner label "Penerimaan hasil" → "Penerimaan produksi"; also in code comments + ERD). Kept unchanged because they describe the goods, not the feature (offered, not decided): form heading "Terima Hasil Produksi", button "Terima hasil", tab "Hasil produksi menunggu diterima", banner "Hasil sudah diterima gudang".
- **Receipt form hint removed (user request):** the dashed side note "Hitung yang benar-benar datang. Posting membuat satu lot…" is gone (key deleted, 554 keys); the form card is now a single `max-w-3xl` card instead of a 2/3 column next to the note.
- **WO detail: shortage + incoming per planned material (user request 2026-10-10, for WO-2026-000022 = the assembly WO).** `plannedMaterials` now also gives `stock`, `shortage` (still needed = planned − issued, minus current stock; only while the WO isn't Completed/Cancelled), `incoming` and `covered` (stock + incoming ≥ still needed). New computed **`incoming()`** per product from both sources: **purchase** = POs Approved / Partially Received, ordered − received (`PurchaseOrderItem::remainingQuantity()`), dated by `expected_delivery_date`; **production** = other WOs making that material that are Released / In Progress (target, by `planned_end_date`) plus posted results not yet received by the warehouse (good qty, "Selesai, menunggu diterima gudang", `ready` flag). Drafts (POs awaiting approval, Draft WOs) excluded — not commitments yet. Sorted by date. Table gains **Stok** (red chip + "kurang X" when short) and **Akan datang** (click → Flux modal per material listing source chip Pembelian/Produksi, PO/WO number, supplier / work center / result, status, quantity, expected or finished date, red "terlambat" when past due). Mobile layout too. Verified: WO-22 → Tutup Termos short 100, incoming 490 from WO-2026-000020 / RES-2026-000007 awaiting receipt ("menutupi kekurangan"); PO source checked with PO-2026-000002 (no date → "tanpa tanggal"). 11 keys (565). Not reserved: incoming, like stock, is a snapshot — another WO may take it first.
- **Redesign of those cells (/ui-ux-pro-max, user screenshot):** the red pill + "kurang 100" caption + orange link + green "menutupi kekurangan" caption (4 visual languages, mixed mono/sans) replaced by: **Stok** and **Akan datang** as plain tabular numbers (Stok red when short; Akan datang dotted-underline button with a chevron, aria-label, focus ring) + a new **Status** column with ONE badge, icon + text (`color-not-only`): ✓ **Cukup** (green), ⏱ **Kurang X · sedang datang** (amber, short but stock + incoming covers it), ⚠ **Kurang X** (red); "—" once the WO is Completed/Cancelled. `plannedMaterials` returns `status` (enough / incoming / short / null) instead of `covered`. Badge is an anonymous component **`<x-material-status :status :shortage :unit>`** (`resources/views/components/material-status.blade.php`), used in desktop and mobile layouts. Keys: removed "covers the shortage", "not enough", "short :qty"; added "Sufficient" (Cukup), "Short :qty" (Kurang :qty), "on the way" (sedang datang). Visual check in the browser still needed.
- **Material Issue form:** "Sudah dikeluarkan" column widened 6.5rem → 9rem + header `whitespace-nowrap` (user screenshot; column order unchanged — the user did not want a reorder).
- **WO detail progress bar redefined (user request 2026-10-10):** was good ÷ target (stayed 0% until the result). Now **0 → 50% as materials are issued** (average per planned material of issued ÷ planned, each capped at 1 — shares, not quantities, since units differ; over-issuing can't exceed a material's share), **100% when the WO is Completed** (result posted); Cancelled / no materials → 0%. Bar turns green at 100%, a tick marks 50%, caption below: "Menunggu bahan" / "Bahan dikeluarkan x/y" (fully issued count) / "Selesai"; `aria-valuetext` includes the stage. Verified on WO-22 by temporarily lowering issued quantities (rolled back): 0/0 → 0%, 50/0 → 12%, 100/0 → 25%, 100/50 → 37%, 100/100 → 50%; completed WOs 100%.
- **Production result "Tanggal selesai" is no longer editable (user request 2026-10-10).** Rule chosen: **completion date = the day the result is posted** (posting = the Kepala Produksi declaring the work finished). Form: `#[Locked] $productionDate` = today, `flux:input type=date disabled` (hint text removed at the user's request); date validation rules removed from the form; save passes today. `PostProductionResultAction` sets `production_date = today()` before re-validating, so a draft saved earlier gets the posting day. Verified (rolled back): input disabled with today's value; setting the property from the browser → `CannotUpdateLockedPropertyException`; a draft dated 2026-10-08 posted with 2026-10-10. The "not before the first issue" rule still runs (always true for today). Key "The completion date cannot be in the future." left in place (still used by `InvalidProductionResultException`).
- **Penerimaan Barang list rebuilt to the warehouse-page convention (user request 2026-10-10)** — same layout as Pengeluaran Barang / Retur Bahan / Penerimaan Produksi: no header button, two tabs. Tab 1 **"PO menunggu barang"**: Approved / Partially Received POs, soonest expected delivery first (no date last), rows with PO number (→ PO receiving history page) + status badge, supplier, expected delivery (red "terlambat" when past), "Barang diterima x/y" progress bar (fully received lines), button **"Terima barang"** → `item-receipts.create/{po}`. Tab 2 **"Dokumen penerimaan"**: RCV receipt batches (number → the PO's history page, since there is no page per RCV; PO + supplier; received at / by; item count), paginated. No money on the list (the old PO-total column is gone). Old segmented status filter + "Create Item Receipt" header button removed. **Sidebar "Penerimaan Barang" now opens the list** (was the create page), active on `item-receipts.*`. Create/detail pages unchanged (their links already pointed at the list). Gate `warehouse.receive` in `mount()`. 7 keys (574). Render-tested both tabs as warehouse (no rupiah) and 403 for production.
- **Cancel WO built (2026-10-13; Next Steps #33 done).** Rules chosen while building: allowed only from **Draft or Released** (status flow; an In Progress WO is finished by posting its result, even if short); **reason required**; who/when/why stored; **material issue drafts of that WO are cancelled with it** (nothing moved). Migration `production/2026_10_13_100000_add_cancel_fields_to_work_orders.php` (`cancelled_by`, `cancelled_at`, `cancel_reason`; guarded + `cancelledBy()` on `WorkOrder`; ERD updated). `CancelWorkOrderAction` (locks the WO, sets the fields, `transitionTo(Cancelled)` — throws for other statuses — then cancels the WO's draft issues). `WorkOrderPolicy::cancel` (production.create + Draft/Released). WO detail: red ghost button "Batalkan Perintah Kerja" → modal with required "Alasan" (+ note about issue drafts when Released) → toast; cancelled WOs show a grey banner "Dibatalkan oleh … · date" + the reason. Print stays blocked for Cancelled (existing policy). Also noted: user has completed WO-2026-000022 (Termos) in their own data.

## Session 21 (2026-10-13) — Cancel WO, Sales module (customers · sales orders · Pengiriman Penjualan), comment cleanup, SO fields to the user's spec, picker fix, master data (products · suppliers · formulas)

Same branch `main`. User committed at the start (after Session 20). Assistant wrote all code (2026-10-05 rule). Cancel WO is logged in Session 20's notes.

- **Dummy account:** `sales@test.com` / `password`, role `sales`, id 10, email verified, created via tinker (not a seeder), like the other test accounts.
- **Decisions (2026-10-13):** (1) products get a **default selling price** that pre-fills SO lines, editable per order; (2) **Sales confirms the SO itself**, no approval; (3) short stock at confirm is **warned only**, showing incoming stock (make-to-order); (4) **several deliveries per SO**, on separate Gudang pages "Pengiriman Penjualan"; (5) customers managed by **Sales and Manager** (no head of sales). Choices made while building: numbers **SO-{year}-{id}**, **DO-{year}-{id}** (surat jalan), customers **CUS-{id}**; only finished goods can be sold; quantities are whole units; Draft → Post for deliveries (like Material Issue), one open delivery draft per SO; no over-delivery; cancel SO only while nothing is delivered (reason required, cancels its delivery drafts); prices exclude tax (tax goes on the invoice, Accounting). No journal yet.
- **Migration** `sales/2026_10_13_110000_create_sales_tables.php`: `products.selling_price` (nullable), `customers`, `sales_orders` (+ notes, confirmed_by/at, cancelled_by/at, cancel_reason), `sales_order_items` (quantity_ordered/delivered/returned, unit_price, subtotal; unique order+product), `sales_deliveries` (+ notes, created_by; delivered_by/at on posting; `document_status`), `sales_delivery_items` (quantity, cogs_total; unique delivery+line). ERD updated to match.
- **Enum** `SalesOrderStatus` (draft → confirmed → partially/fully delivered; cancel from draft/confirmed; `isDeliverable()`). Models `Customer`, `SalesOrder` (`transitionTo`, `ensureIsDraft`, `syncDeliveryStatus()`, `hasDeliveries()`), `SalesOrderItem` (`remainingQuantity()`, `isFullyDelivered()`), `SalesDelivery`, `SalesDeliveryItem` (`stockMovements()` morph). Morph key **`sales_delivery_item`**. Gates **`sales.manage`** (Sales, Manager) and **`warehouse.deliver`** (Warehouse, Manager); policies `CustomerPolicy`, `SalesOrderPolicy` (update/confirm = draft; cancel = draft/confirmed), `SalesDeliveryPolicy`.
- **Refactors (one rule, one place):**
  - **`App\Actions\Inventory\DrawStockFifoAction`** — the FIFO draw (`lockAvailableLots()`, `available()`, `draw()`: oldest lot first, one OUT movement per lot, emptying draw takes the whole remaining value). `PostMaterialIssueAction` now uses it (regression-tested: same cost/movements, invariants hold, short stock still blocked); `PostSalesDeliveryAction` uses it too.
  - **`App\Support\IncomingStock::forProducts($ids, exceptWorkOrderId)`** + `total()` — the incoming lookup moved out of the WO detail page (which now calls it); the SO form/detail use it.
- **Actions:** `App\Actions\Sales\SaveCustomerAction`, `SaveSalesOrderDraftAction` (FG only, replaces lines, recomputes `total_amount`), `ConfirmSalesOrderAction`, `CancelSalesOrderAction`; `App\Actions\Warehouse\SaveSalesDeliveryDraftAction` (static `checkDeliverable()`: order deliverable, no other draft, ≥1 line, ≤ remaining per line), **`PostSalesDeliveryAction`** (locks delivery + SO, re-checks, locks lots and checks every line before drawing, draws FIFO with type `SalesShipment` → `cogs_total`, adds to `quantity_delivered`, posts, `syncDeliveryStatus()`), `CancelSalesDeliveryAction`. Exceptions `InvalidSalesOrderException`, `InvalidSalesDeliveryException`, `InvalidSalesOrderStatusTransitionException`, `SalesOrderNotEditableException`, `SalesDeliveryNotEditableException`.
- **Pages:** `sales/customers/⚡list` (Employees-style table + create/edit modal), `sales/orders/⚡list` (status tabs with counts, delivered x/y, late flag), `sales/orders/⚡form` (create + edit draft; customer picker, expected delivery, notes, product rows with price pre-filled from `selling_price`, stock + incoming hint per row, live total), `sales/orders/⚡show` (customer/info, lines with stock / incoming (modal) / `<x-material-status>` badge — stock already promised to other open orders is subtracted first —, deliveries history, Confirm modal with shortage warning, Cancel modal with reason). Gudang `warehouse/outbound/sales-deliveries/⚡list` (tab "Pesanan menunggu dikirim" with products-delivered progress + "Kirim barang" / "Buka draf"; tab "Dokumen pengiriman"), `⚡form` (ordered / already delivered / remaining / stock / send now pre-filled with remaining), `⚡show` (lot trail; COGS only with `costs.view`). Sidebar: Penjualan → Pesanan Penjualan, Pelanggan; Gudang → Pengiriman Penjualan. 89 translation keys (672).
