## Session 35 (2026-10-26) — Manager dashboard (Dasbor Manajemen) + DemoSeeder — ✅ DONE

Uncommitted. Assistant wrote all code, no code comments. The user approved the proposed metric set ("build all of it, with month picker and seeder") and asked for graphs instead of tables.

**Page** `pages::executive.dashboard` (`resources/views/pages/executive/⚡dashboard.blade.php`) replaces the `dashboards.placeholder` view on `/executive/dashboard`. The month picker is `#[Url] period`, like the production dashboard, and every trend covers the 6 months ending at that month. Contents:
- **Tiles** (each with a server-side sparkline, `resources/views/components/sparkline.blade.php`, and the change vs last month): Penjualan, Laba kotor (margin), Laba bersih, Kas & bank, Nilai persediaan, Piutang lewat jatuh tempo (with an overdue-share meter). Each tile links to its report.
- **Perlu perhatian:** a two-column list showing the current state whatever month is chosen: POs pending approval, overdue customer invoices, supplier invoices due within 7 days, material shortages (`MaterialShortage`), late WOs, last month's overhead not closed, unmapped system roles.
- **Keuangan:** sales and HPP bars with a gross-profit line (new `groupedBar` chart; tooltip shows margin %); cash at each month end (`lineTrend`).
- **Penjualan & piutang:** margin per product (HTML bars, cost + profit or loss, from posted deliveries in the month: qty × SO price × (1 − SO discount) vs `cogs_total`); sales per customer donut (top 5 + Lainnya, invoice DPP); receivables aging (`resources/views/executive/_aging.blade.php`: buckets not due / 1–30 / 31–60 / 61–90 / > 90 as of the month end or today, plus the 3 most overdue customers).
- **Persediaan & pembelian:** inventory value by type donut (ledger balances of the 4 inventory roles at month end); stock age stacked bars (lots on hand **today**, by `received_at`); payables aging.
- **Produksi:** overhead applied vs actual per month (`groupedBar`, closing journals excluded); WO pipeline (open now + completed in the month) and labor + overhead vs plan (reuses `ProductionKpis::completedWorkOrders()`).
- **Provisional notice:** when the chosen month's overhead isn't closed yet but has applied overhead, an amber banner and the gross/net tile notes say the month's profit is provisional (applied BOP already credits HPP; actual bills come at closing).

**Data:** `App\Support\Kpi\ExecutiveKpis` provides `profitTrend()` (`IncomeStatementReport` per month), `balanceTrend()` (cash + inventory per type at each month end), `overheadTrend()`, `overheadOpen()`, `productMargins()`, `customerShare()`, `receivablesAging()`, `payablesAging()`, `stockAge()`, `workOrderPipeline()`, `costVsPlan()`, and static `attention()`. **"Cash" accounts = the accounts used as `cash_account_id` on any sales or purchase payment.** There is no `is_cash` flag, so Kas/Bank only count once a payment has used them; an `is_cash` flag would be needed for a proper Arus Kas.
**JS** (`resources/js/charts.js`): new `groupedBar` (side-by-side bars + optional line series + `notes` tooltip rows) and `donut` (click to isolate, tooltip with share). `lineTrend` gained a wider axis for `compactRupiah`, `overallColor` and `labelFormat`. 90 `lang/id.json` keys.

**DemoSeeder** (`database/seeders/DemoSeeder.php`) is **for a fresh, separate database only** (it calls the master seeders and creates users). Run it with `DB_DATABASE=mims_demo php artisan migrate:fresh --seed --seeder=DemoSeeder`, never on `tugas_akhir`. Built in database `mims_demo` (already seeded). It simulates PT SAI day by day from 1 May 2026 to the day it runs, with `Date::setTestNow()` on each day and **the real Actions**, so journals, FIFO lots, stock and statuses are produced exactly as by the app:
- **Setup:** 6 users (`manager@test.com`, `purchasing@`, `sales@`, `accounting@`, `production@`, `warehouse@`; password `password`); 3 FG lines Termos 1L Merah/Biru/Hijau (48k/46k/42k) with their own badan WIP and a shared tutup; 10 employees; 31 accounts incl. Bank BCA, machines/molds and contra accumulated depreciation; 5 customers with different terms and payment habits (UD Sinar Terang pays 45–70 days late, PT Grosir Nusantara 12–30 days late).
- **Opening balance (1 May):** Kas 25 jt, Bank 250 jt, Mesin 600 jt, Molding 150 jt / Modal 1.025 jt.
- **Weekly cycle:** Mon POs (approved by the manager) + sales orders; Wed receive POs (invoice +2 days, paid at due date + supplier delay) and start molding WOs; Thu molding results + deliveries + invoices (payments at due date + customer delay); Fri assembly WOs; results the next day (about 1 in 9 a day late).
- **Month end:** actual overhead (electricity scales with machine hours), the closing, operating expenses, and wage payment.
- **Story it creates:** demand grows 1.500 → 2.600 termos a month; PP Merah price rises on 1 Aug; PP Nilek bought in May is never used (slow stock).
- **Left open on purpose for the alerts:** 2 POs pending approval, 1 WO stuck in progress, and a Draft WO that causes material shortages.

**Verified:** in `mims_demo`, the trial balance and Neraca balance in every month May–Oct; ledger = lots for all 3 inventory accounts; `current_stock` = Σ lots for every product; revenue May 66 jt → Oct 107 jt; net 6.8 → 18.9 jt (Sep); margins Merah 38.9% / Biru 38.5% / Hijau 32.4%. Screenshots checked, live, light and dark, Oct (provisional) and Sep (closed).
**To view the demo:** run a second server, `DB_DATABASE=mims_demo APP_URL=http://127.0.0.1:8001 php artisan serve --port=8001 --no-reload` (`--no-reload` is needed, otherwise the child process re-reads `.env` and uses `tugas_akhir`), or switch `DB_DATABASE` in `.env`. While testing, two stale `php` processes found listening on port 8001 (started 18 Oct and 26 Oct 13:10, before this session; most likely old `php artisan serve` fallbacks from `composer run dev`) were stopped; the 8000 server was untouched.

**KPI row redesign (same day, /ui-ux-pro-max, user: "having all the cards being line graph a bit bad").** The 6 identical sparkline tiles were replaced by 4 panels, each with a form that fits its question. This follows MASTER.md's own anti-pattern (no generic KPI stat-card rows). Grid `lg:grid-cols-12`:
- **Penjualan** (7 columns): hero number + change vs last month; a 6-month column chart drawn in HTML (current month in accent, others muted, compact value labels); "Ke mana setiap Rp 100 penjualan", a 100% split bar of HPP / Beban usaha / Laba bersih (or Rugi in red), with Rp-per-100 and amounts; amber "provisional" line while the month's overhead is open.
- **Kas & bank** (5 columns, top): balance + change, then a bridge (cash now + still to collect from customers − still to pay suppliers = net position) as thin bars.
- **Below it, side by side:** **Piutang lewat jatuh tempo** (SVG ring gauge with the overdue share, amount, count, total owed) and **Nilai persediaan** (value, "≈ N hari penjualan" = inventory ÷ average daily HPP of the last 3 closed months, and a split bar by type with %).

**Narrow-width fixes (user screenshot of a squeezed "Posisi kas" card):** `chartBase.drawXLabels` now skips x-labels when they'd collide (keeps the last one, every n-th backwards), which applies to every chart; `lineTrend` takes `endLabel: 'above'` (value label above the last point, plot uses the full width; used for the cash chart); the cash figure and its change note wrap instead of breaking the number. The KPI row's side-by-side layout moved from `lg` to `xl` (below xl: Penjualan full width, then cash | receivables + inventory). Stock-age buckets use short labels (0–30 … > 90) so none get skipped. Checked at 1100 and 1440 px.
**Laba rugi card (user: "i dont see a one part that give laporan laba rugi data"):** a full-width card in Keuangan, under the sales/cash charts, fed by `ExecutiveKpis::incomeStatement()` (= `IncomeStatementReport::build()` for the month, so it always matches the report).
- **Left:** a horizontal waterfall: Penjualan → − HPP → Laba kotor (margin %) → − each Beban usaha account (top 3 + "Beban lainnya" when more than 4) → Laba bersih/Rugi bersih (margin %). Bars are positioned on one shared scale, with a zero line when the domain goes negative.
- **Right:** year-to-date totals (Penjualan, Laba kotor %, Beban usaha, Laba bersih %) and net profit for each of the last 6 months (bars above or below a zero line).
- The provisional note shows while overhead is open; there is a "Laporan laba rugi lengkap" link.

Donut legends: names left-aligned, amounts never wrap. The KPI row's side-by-side layout now starts at a custom `min-[1400px]` breakpoint (at 1280 the small cards overflowed).
**Swap (user: "switch nilai persediaan with laporan laba rugi since there is already persediaan in Persediaan & pembelian"):** the "Nilai persediaan" KPI card was removed (inventory stays in its own section), and the Laba rugi card moved up to sit directly under the KPI row. Page order is now: KPI row (Penjualan | Kas & bank + Piutang lewat jatuh tempo) → Laba rugi → Perlu perhatian → Keuangan → Penjualan & piutang → Persediaan & pembelian → Produksi. The right column uses `sm:max-[1399px]:grid-cols-2` (a bounded range); `lg:grid-cols-2 min-[1400px]:grid-cols-1` didn't work because Tailwind orders the `lg:` rule after the arbitrary `min-[…]` one, so `lg` won. 7 now-unused translation keys removed.
"Laba bersih per bulan" bars now carry value labels (`formatShort()`: `833rb`, `18,9jt`, `−22,2jt`): above the tip for profit, below in red for a loss, `0` on the baseline. Head and foot room are reserved so labels never clip. Checked with a temporary loss journal in `mims_demo` (removed again).
`components/sparkline.blade.php` deleted (unused). Translations: 21 keys added, 9 now-unused keys removed. Checked at 1440 px light/dark and at 390 px (stacks to one column).

## Session 36 (2026-10-26) — Pembayaran Upah (wage payments) — ✅ DONE

Uncommitted. Assistant wrote all code, no code comments. Closes the roadmap's Layer 1 "wage payments" (Utang Gaji used to only grow).

**Decisions (user):** pay **per employee** (not a lump sum against the Utang Gaji balance), and a payment covers **everything earned up to a chosen cut-off date** (fits weekly, bi-weekly or monthly pay; not a fixed monthly period). Assistant defaults: Akuntansi + Manager only (`wages.pay`); a payment is posted immediately after a journal-confirmation dialog (like other payments, no draft); cancel = reversing journal + hours become unpaid again; number `UPH-{year}-{id:06}`; cash account rule = active asset not in `RecordSalesPaymentAction::reservedRoles()`.

**Rule found while building:** only labor whose production result **has a posted journal** can be paid (`UnpaidWages::query()` uses `whereExists` on `journal_entries` source `production_result`). Results posted before the journal system (before 19 Oct) never credited Utang Gaji; paying them would drive Utang Gaji negative. In dev: posted labor 5.192.000, but payable = 1.784.000 = Utang Gaji balance exactly.

**Built:**
- **Migration** `accounting/2026_10_26_120000_create_wage_payments_tables.php` (runs with plain `php artisan migrate` since Session 34):
  - `wage_payments` (payment_date, `period_end` cut-off, cash_account_id, total_amount, reference, status posted/cancelled, created/cancelled by+at).
  - `wage_payment_lines` (employee, hours, amount; unique payment+employee).
  - **`production_result_labors.wage_payment_id`** (nullable, nullOnDelete). It marks a row as paid, so nothing is paid twice; cancel sets it back to null.
- **Models:** `WagePayment`, `WagePaymentLine`; `ProductionResultLabor::wagePayment()`.
- **Support:** `App\Support\UnpaidWages` (`query(until)`, `perEmployee(until)` → employee, hours, amount, results, from/to dates; `total(until)`).
- **Actions:** `PayWagesAction::handle(periodEnd, paymentDate, cashAccountId, employeeIds, reference, userId)` locks the unpaid rows, writes the lines, stamps the rows and journals **Dr Utang Gaji / Cr Kas-Bank** on the payment date; static `plan()`. `CancelWagePaymentAction`. Exception `InvalidWagePaymentException` (NO_EMPLOYEES, NOTHING_TO_PAY, FUTURE_DATE, PERIOD_AFTER_PAYMENT, NOT_CASH_ACCOUNT, ALREADY_CANCELLED).
- **Wiring:** gate `wages.pay`; morph key `wage_payment`; routes `accounting.wages.index` (`/accounting/wages`), `.pay`, `.show`; sidebar Akuntansi → "Pembayaran Upah" (icon wallet).
- **Pages** `accounting/wages/`:
  - `⚡index`: unpaid per employee with bars, plus a "Utang gaji di pembukuan" card that checks the ledger balance against the unpaid hours and explains a difference (a manual journal on Utang Gaji); payment history table.
  - `⚡pay`: cut-off + payment date, employee table with checkboxes (all selected; re-selected when the cut-off changes; Pilih semua / Kosongkan), live total, cash account picker, reference, then a confirm modal with the journal preview.
  - `⚡show`: tiles, lines per employee with chips linking to each paid production result (number · date · hours), journal links, cancel modal with the reversal preview.
- 47 `lang/id.json` keys; ERD: both tables, the new column, TableGroup Akuntansi.
- **DemoSeeder** now pays wages through `PayWagesAction` (cut-off = month end, paid on the 1st, Bank BCA) instead of a manual journal. `mims_demo` reseeded: 5 payments May–Sep (106.525.300); October unpaid 24.987.500 = Utang Gaji; all other figures unchanged.

**Verified (rolled back, ID counters reset):** logic 20/20 (partial selection, journal, Utang Gaji = unpaid after each step, double pay refused, future date / cut-off after payment date / no employees / inventory account refused, cancel restores everything, cancel twice refused, re-pay after cancel); pages 16/16 (Produksi 403 on index and pay, preselection + totals, cut-off change, validation, post + redirect, show chips/journal, cancel, sidebar). Screenshots checked (index, pay, show with demo data).

## Concepts Already Taught (don't re-explain unless asked)
- **(Session 25) Opening balances belong together, not one at a time:** posting a single account's starting balance in isolation (e.g. `Dr Kas 1.000.000`) forces a guessed equity plug for the credit side every time, because there's nothing real to net it against. The correct cutover is one entry with every known asset/liability balance as its own line; only the genuine leftover (assets − liabilities) goes to equity (Modal/Laba Ditahan) — a real figure then, not a per-account guess.
- **(Session 25) Journal entry line mechanics:** per line, debit and credit are mutually exclusive (both filled = "debit and credit the same account," cancels to nothing); across the whole entry both sides are required, but that falls out of the balance check for free (an all-one-side entry can never total to zero on both sides, so there's no separate "needs both" rule to write). The Jurnal Umum create page's Post button is disabled live the entire time the running totals don't match — the actual guarantee; `PostJournalEntryAction`'s own balance check is a defense-in-depth backstop, not the primary gate.
- **(Session 22) Overhead in one picture:** three cost elements — bahan baku and tenaga kerja are traceable per WO, overhead is not (one PLN bill, machine wear) — so overhead is charged like **renting the machine per hour**: rate = estimated monthly overhead ÷ budgeted machine hours, set in advance because the bills arrive after the month; each WO pays actual machine hours × rate (applied); after the month, applied vs actual = under/over-applied, corrected in one closing journal (all to HPP), never by re-costing documents or lots. A month's profit is final only after that closing.
- **(Session 22) Machine hour = cost driver:** one machine running one hour; a measure of activity, not a cost. Electricity, depreciation, mold wear and maintenance all grow with run time, so they are shared by machine hours (labor hours would overcharge the assembly line ×4; pieces would ignore that a body takes longer than a lid). A work center's rate = cost of all its machines ÷ hours of all its machines = one machine-hour; valid only while its machines are alike — different machines → separate work centers.
- **(Session 22) Splitting one shared bill (electricity):** by machine hours (simple, overcharges low-power equipment) vs. by estimated kWh = kW × hours (fairer) vs. sub-meters (exact). The split only matters for the estimate; actuals and the variance are factory-wide, so a split error moves cost between work centers but never loses money.
- **(Session 22) Entry date vs. period:** a cost belongs to the month it was incurred, not the day it was typed in — so documents carry a `period` separate from `created_at`, and a period stays open for late bills until it is closed.
- **(Session 22) Price vs. cost at the point of decision:** cost is shown where prices are chosen (Manager's default price, Sales' SO line) with the margin = (price − cost) ÷ price; the SO preview follows the same FIFO order as the real delivery and skips stock already promised to other confirmed orders — one ordering rule (`InventoryLot::availableFifo`) for both.
- **(Session 22) PPN Keluaran vs. PPN Masukan:** tax belongs on the **invoice**, not the order (orders change, deliver partially, get cancelled; the faktur pajak goes with the invoice). Output VAT on a sale is a **liability** (Dr Piutang / Cr Penjualan / Cr PPN Keluaran — revenue stays tax-free); input VAT on a purchase is an **asset** credited against it (Dr Barang Diterima Belum Difakturkan / Dr PPN Masukan / Cr Utang Usaha); monthly Keluaran − Masukan is paid to the tax office. For a PKP, PPN Masukan is **not** inventory cost, so FIFO lots / WO cost / COGS never change because of tax. Rate: 12% on a tax base of 11/12 for ordinary goods = effectively 11%.
- **(Session 22) Dependency order of master data:** a row can only be created after everything it points to exists (formula → product, formula item → material product), which gives the bottom-up setup order raw → WIP → WIP formula → FG → FG formula.
- **(Session 22) Server rule vs. UI convenience:** a disabled field (unit locked to Pcs, export button hidden) is only convenience; the real guarantee is the Action / Policy that refuses the wrong value or request, because a request can be sent without the page. Same reason the PDF route re-checks `export` even though the button is hidden.
- **(Session 22) Don't relabel a counted quantity:** changing the unit of a product that already has stock (100 Kg → "100 Pcs") silently changes what the stock means — hence `isLocked()` and the guard in the Pcs migration.
- **(Session 21) Revenue vs COGS:** a sale has two values that never mix — the price the customer pays (SO line, chosen by Sales) and the cost of the goods that left (COGS, decided by FIFO from the lots at delivery). Gross margin = revenue − COGS. The SO is about revenue; the delivery is about cost — which is why COGS lives on the delivery line, not the order.
- **(Session 20) Where a product's cost lives:** the production result computes the cost; the production receipt turns it into a **lot** — and FIFO only knows lots. That's how Badan Termos's cost travels into the assembly WO: Material Issue draws the Badan Termos lot at the value the receipt gave it. A shortage at receipt is booked as a loss rather than raising the lot's unit cost, so downstream products carry what production really cost.
- **(Session 20) One rule, one place:** the leftover's value (used by the production result to price what was NOT used) and the lots a return refills (used by Material Return) are the same walk — `reverseDraws()`. If they were computed separately they could drift by a rupiah and the WO cost + returned value + variance would no longer add up to what was issued.
- **(Session 19) When to take a snapshot:** a copy (rates, recipe) should be taken at the moment the document becomes a commitment, not when it is first typed. A Draft WO is still a plan, so its copied rates are provisional and refreshed at release; after release they're frozen. Same idea as costs being fixed only when a production result is posted.
- **(Session 19) Validate on save AND on post:** a draft can sit for days; between saving and posting, issues get posted, rates change, employees are deactivated. So the rules live in one shared Action (`ValidateProductionResultAction`) called by both, and posting recomputes everything under row locks instead of trusting what the draft stored.
- **(Session 19) Need-to-know / least privilege for data, not just pages:** a role can be allowed to open a page yet not see every field on it. Here one gate (`costs.view`) hides rupiah amounts inside warehouse pages; the FIFO costing still runs on posting, nobody has to see the numbers for it to work. Livewire public model properties are sent to the browser as identifiers only, so hiding in the Blade is enough as long as no public array property carries costs. *(Widened 2026-10-15: Sales may now see costs too — `costs.view` = Akuntansi, Manager, Sales; Warehouse and Production still see none.)*
- **(Session 18) `stock_movements` ↔ `inventory_lots` and FIFO:** lots are the mutable per-batch balance, movements the immutable ledger (one OUT row per lot drawn). Three invariants: `current_stock` = Σ IN − Σ OUT = Σ lot `quantity_remaining`; per lot `quantity_remaining` = `quantity_initial` − Σ its OUT movements. The draw that empties a lot takes its whole `value_remaining` so rounding never strands rupiah.
- **(Session 18) How WIP gets its unit cost:** (material at FIFO + labor + overhead) ÷ good quantity; normal rejects are absorbed by good units, abnormal rejects go to `abnormal_loss_cost`; store total value, not only the unit cost.
- **(Session 18) Why `material_issue_item` is in the morph map:** `stock_movements.reference_type/reference_id` is polymorphic (one FK column pointing at different tables). The map stores a stable key instead of the PHP class name, and works in both directions: `morphMany` builds its `WHERE reference_type = …` from the map (a missing entry silently returns nothing, e.g. an empty FIFO trail), and `morphTo` resolves the key back to a class. Movements point at the *line*, not the document, because one movement = one product from one lot.
- **(Session 18) `quantity` vs `quantity_consumed` on `material_issue_items`:** `quantity` = what left the warehouse (set at posting); `quantity_consumed` = what production actually used (set by the production result). The difference is material on the floor, out of `current_stock` but not yet in any product. Both cost columns are system-computed, hence guarded.
- **(Session 18) A Material Return as an exact undo of FIFO draws:** refilling the last-drawn lot first leaves stock exactly as if only the consumed amount had been drawn by FIFO; the returned value is priced at those lots' own costs, so WO material cost must be issued − returned, not consumed × average cost (the average would leave a small rupiah gap between warehouse and WIP).
- **Effective-dated records** (labor rates, overhead rates): never UPDATE a rate — close the open one the day before the new one starts and insert a new row; "rate on date X" = started on/before X and not yet ended. Backdated rates are rejected because they'd overlap covered periods.
- **Snapshot vs. lookup**: a document copies the values it was approved with (WO: `hourly_rate`, `overhead_rate`, `work_center_id`, `work_order_materials`; PO: `unit_price`) so later master-data edits can't silently change it. A formula is a template used once at WO creation.
- **Derive, don't accept**: values derivable from other data (WO `product_id`/`work_center_id` from the formula, `created_by` from Auth) are never taken from the caller — nothing can contradict them.
- **A rule enforced at every step that depends on it** (`<<include>>` labor checked at create AND at release) — the step shouldn't trust that every path into it remembered.
- **Locking a parent row to serialize changes** when the thing to protect isn't one row (employee row while changing its open labor rate; work center row for overhead rates).
- **Deactivate instead of delete** for master data referenced by history (employees inactive, FKs `restrictOnDelete`).
- **Codes vs. document numbers**: dated documents carry a year (`WO-2026-…`, `RCV-2026-…`); master records don't (`EMP-000001`).
- **Reference data the schema depends on belongs in the migration** (work centers inserted by the migration that a later migration's required FK needs); sample data belongs in seeders.
- **Cost driver**: overhead follows the activity that causes it — machine hours, not labor hours (assembly: 32 labor h vs. 8 machine h).
- **Predetermined rate is an estimate even on actuals**: production results apply *actual hours × estimated rate*; only `overhead_expenses` are fully actual; the month-end variance reconciles them.
- **Warn at planning, enforce at execution** (material shortage chips on WO create; real stock check when materials are issued).
- **Prevent mistakes instead of reporting them** (already-chosen workers/products unpickable) — server validation and DB constraints stay as the safety net.
- **`<flux:error>` ignores its slot** — pass `:message="$message"` (or use `name="field"`). General lesson: when a vendor component silently does nothing, read its source.
- **Native `<select>` can't be styled per option** (browser/OS draws it) → custom listbox; you must re-add keyboard handling + ARIA (`listbox`/`option`/`aria-selected`) yourself.
- **A Blade component can't call the Livewire page's methods** (`$this` is the component) — pass prepared data in; `wire:click` inside still targets the enclosing Livewire component.
- **`<table>` shrinks columns to fit; a grid with fixed tracks + `minmax(0,1fr)` doesn't** — use grids for rows of inputs. **Container queries** (`@container` / `@3xl:`) adapt to the box's own width, not the viewport — right for components living in a narrow column.
- **Grid rows stretch cells by default** → a field with an error grows and drags its neighbour; `items-start` fixes it.
- **A CSS `transform` on an ancestor** (e.g. an animation with `fill-mode: both`) makes `position: fixed` children relative to that ancestor and creates a stacking context (later siblings can paint over popups) → use `backwards` fill so the transform is gone after the animation. **`overflow: auto/hidden` clips absolutely positioned popups.**
- **Don't `x-teleport` things Livewire re-renders**: Livewire re-syncs the teleported copy from server HTML and wipes Alpine's inline styles.
- **Don't rely on a DB default for a value code reads right after insert** — set it explicitly or `refresh()` (WO `status`).
- **`migrate:rollback` needs `--path` for subfolder migrations; `--step` counts migrations, not batches.** Edit a migration in place only while it's uncommitted (and roll back everything after it); once committed, add a new migration.
- **Livewire test harness**: authorization failures become a 403 response (`assertForbidden()`), not a thrown exception; render tests verify markup/behaviour, not visual layout.
- Predetermined (applied) overhead vs. actual overhead: actual bills/depreciation arrive monthly, but a production lot's `unit_cost` is stored at receipt time, so overhead must be applied immediately with a budgeted rate (BOP Dibebankan); the gap with actual is closed at period end as under/over-applied overhead (selisih BOP). Allocating actual overhead after the fact would mean rewriting costs of lots already consumed/sold downstream.
- Plan vs. actual are stored side by side, never overwritten (`work_order_labors` plan vs. `production_result_labors` actual; `quantity_target` vs. `quantity_good`) — variance analysis needs both numbers to survive.
- Composite (multi-column) unique constraints — the DB rejects duplicate *combinations*, regardless of which code path inserts them; each column alone can still repeat (e.g. the same material in different formulas). Used to make "one version number per product" and "one line per material per recipe" guarantees rather than conventions.
- MySQL caps identifier names (index/constraint names) at 64 characters; Laravel's auto-generated composite index names can exceed it — pass an explicit short name as the second argument. MySQL DDL isn't transactional, so a migration that fails mid-way can leave a half-created table behind that must be dropped manually.
- Data migrations should use `DB::table()` rather than Eloquent models — a migration is a frozen point in schema history, while models keep changing (renames, guarded fields, observers), which could silently change or break an old migration's behavior on a fresh install.
- Adding a NOT NULL + UNIQUE column to a table that already has rows: add nullable → backfill → tighten with `->change()`.
- MySQL auto-increment counters are not rolled back with a transaction — rolled-back inserts leave permanent gaps in id-derived document numbers.
- Input validation vs. business validation vs. authorization (three distinct layers)
- Services vs. Actions (workflow orchestration vs. atomic operation)
- Events/Listeners for decoupled side effects
- Why cached/derived fields (stock, totals) must be calculated, not manually set
- Row locking (`lockForUpdate()`) and transactions for concurrency safety, as the actual fix for concurrent-stock-edit risk (instead of real-time UI)
- Real-time backend cache consistency vs. real-time UI push — two separate concepts, only the former is needed
- Service Container, bindings, and Service Providers (`register()` vs `boot()`) — used to override Fortify's `LoginResponse` without touching vendor code
- Gate vs. Middleware (rule definition vs. enforcement checkpoint) — and Gate vs. Policy (model-less vs. model-tied permission)
- `Fillable` (incoming mass-assignment whitelist) vs. `Hidden` (outgoing serialization blacklist) — two independent concerns
- `#[Guarded([...])]` used specifically to lock out *system-derived* fields (stock, status, totals, subtotals) from mass assignment, distinct from `#[Fillable]` being a general whitelist
  - **Re-explained in Session 19 on `ProductionResult`** (user asked why so many fields are guarded): rule of thumb **typed by the user = fillable; decided by the system = guarded**: `id`, `result_number` (generated), `status` (only via `transitionTo()`), the five cost fields (computed on posting), `posted_by`/`posted_at` (set at posting). Save passes user input as an array (`$result->update($fields)`), Post assigns system values one by one. Why bother when the Action already passes the right fields: **defense in depth**. Livewire public properties can be tampered with in the browser, and a future `update($request->all())` would otherwise let `unit_cost` or `status` be written directly, skipping the calculation and the transition rules.
- Enum-driven finite state machine: the enum owns the legal transition graph (`canTransitionTo`), the model owns enforcement through one single mutator method (`transitionTo`) — why this beats letting any code path do `update(['status' => ...])` directly
- Why bcmath (`bccomp`/`bcsub`/`bcmul`) is used instead of native float comparison/arithmetic for money and quantities — binary floating point rounding error compounds across a ledger
- Custom Pivot models (`extends Pivot`, `#[Table(...)]`, wired via `->using()`) for many-to-many relationships that carry real data, vs. Laravel's default anonymous pivot
- FK delete behavior chosen deliberately per relationship (`cascadeOnDelete` vs `restrictOnDelete` vs `nullOnDelete`) rather than one default across the schema
- Laravel 13 removed `unsignedDecimal`/`unsignedFloat`/`unsignedDouble` from the migration Blueprint — use `->decimal(...)->unsigned()` instead
- `php artisan migrate --path=...` scopes which files are considered, but only runs ones not already recorded in the `migrations` table — need `migrate:rollback --path=...` first to re-run an already-applied file
- Guarding (`#[Guarded]`) blocks *mass assignment* specifically (`fill()`/`update()`/constructor array), not direct property writes — a model's own internal methods can and should bypass it via `$this->field = $value; $this->save();` when the field is guarded precisely to keep external callers from setting it directly
- DTOs are only worth it past ~5 related fields (project's own threshold) — `UpdatePurchaseOrderAction` (3 fields) and the item Actions (2-3 fields) deliberately stayed plain method params instead of getting DTO classes
- Action vs. Service litmus test: if the method's job needs "...and then it also..." to describe it — i.e. it composes other Actions or crosses module boundaries — it's a Service; if it's producing one aggregate in one sentence, it's an Action
- Larastan's method-style `casts()` support needs `parseModelCastsMethod: true` in `phpstan.neon` — off by default
- `local` vs `public` filesystem disk — `public` is web-reachable by anyone with the URL (no auth check), `local` isn't; an internal auth-gated app should default private and serve files through an authorized route, not the convenient-but-wrong `public` disk
- Livewire's `WithFileUploads` uploads eagerly to a temp location the moment a file is picked (not on form submit) — different from a plain HTML form's `$request->file()`, easy to get wrong if reasoning from plain-Laravel-forms intuition
- Why gitignored `storage/app/*` is safe from `git pull` by construction, but not safe from `git clean -fdx`/`rm -rf` inside a deploy script, and why atomic fresh-checkout-per-release deploy tools (Forge/Envoyer-style) need `storage/` explicitly symlinked from a shared persistent directory or every release starts empty
- Denormalizing a column (e.g. `product_id` on a lot table also reachable via a longer FK chain) is a reasonable, low-risk call specifically when the field is write-once at creation and the direct-query path is the actual hot path, not a rare report — this codebase already has a precedent for it (`stock_movements.product_id`), used as the justification rather than re-deriving the tradeoff from scratch
- "Batch" vs "lot" as two genuinely different concepts that must not share a name in this codebase: a receiving *batch* groups multiple items from one delivery visit (for attachments/grouping); a *lot* is the per-product-per-receipt costing/quantity unit
- Livewire lifecycle hooks (`mount()`, `updating()`, `updated()`, `updated{Property}()`) are **magic hooks by convention, not PHP-reserved words** — Livewire's base `Component` class checks whether your class defines a method with that exact name and calls it automatically at the right point, the same pattern as a Service Provider's `boot()`. You never call them yourself.
- `wire:model` vs `wire:model.live` is a **timing** difference, not a different sync mechanism: `.live` fires a request immediately on change/input; plain `wire:model` defers the value until *some* request happens for any reason (another `.live` field, a button click, etc.), at which point it rides along and syncs then. `updated()` fires for both — `.live` just controls *when*.
- `updated{PropertyName}()` (e.g. `updatedSupplierId()`) only works for plain top-level properties — Livewire generates that method name directly from the property name. Dotted array paths like `items.0.product_id` have no valid PHP method-name equivalent, so they only reach the generic `updated(string $name, mixed $value)` catch-all, matched via string/regex on `$name`.
- How a Blade `wire:model.live="items.{{ $index }}.product_id"` (inside a `@foreach`) links to the PHP `updated()` hook: there's no function call between them — Blade substitutes `{{ $index }}` at render time so each row's real HTML has a literal attribute like `wire:model.live="items.0.product_id"`; the browser reads that exact string off the changed element and sends it in the network request; Livewire uses it both to sync `$this->items[0]['product_id']` (via a dot-notation setter) *and* as the `$name` argument passed into `updated()`. One shared string does all three jobs.
- `updated()` is called **once per changed property, per request** — if a single request happens to carry multiple dirty properties (e.g. a deferred `wire:model` field riding along with a `.live` field's request), the hook runs multiple times in that cycle, once per property, each with its own `$name`/`$value`. This is why the regex guard inside it matters: the same hook sees every property change project-wide, not just the one you're targeting.
- `WithPagination`'s `resetPage()` is a thin wrapper over `setPage(1, $pageName)`, which sets `$this->paginators[$pageName] = 1` (an array, not a plain property — `$pageName` lets a component run multiple independent paginators). Calling it inside an `updating{Property}()` hook (e.g. `updatingSearch()`) guards against landing on a now-out-of-range page number after a filter shrinks the result set.
- `#[Url]` and `WithPagination`'s own `page` query-string registration are the *same underlying mechanism* (Livewire's query-string sync) applied two ways — one explicit via attribute, one automatic via the trait's `queryStringHandlesPagination()`. Both make component state bookmarkable/refresh-safe by syncing to the browser URL via `pushState`, not a full reload.
- **"Magic method" precision:** `updatingSearch()`, `updated()`, `mount()`, `rules()` etc. are *not* real PHP magic methods (`__get`/`__set`/etc., which the PHP engine itself recognizes). They're Livewire's own userland convention: `method_exists($this, $constructedName)` checks sprinkled through Livewire's own source, calling your method *in addition to*, not *instead of*, its normal internal behavior if found. Purely additive — never assume defining one "overrides" framework behavior.
- `dd()` is "dump and **die**" — it terminates the PHP process immediately after dumping. Fine for confirming a value in isolation, but leaving it inside a Livewire action method means the request never finishes rendering/responding — a symptom that looks exactly like "the UI isn't updating" is actually "the response never arrived because the script died mid-request." Remove it the moment the value is confirmed.
- A Laravel/Livewire project only needs **one** Alpine.js instance — Livewire bundles and auto-starts its own. Manually `import Alpine from 'alpinejs'; Alpine.start()` in `app.js` creates a second, independent instance racing the first to initialize on every page load, causing exactly the kind of order-dependent, hard-to-reproduce bugs (Flux's appearance/theme store silently losing its persisted value on some loads but not others) that don't throw errors because the two instances don't share Alpine's own duplicate-instance detection. To add an Alpine plugin safely, hook into Livewire's own instance via `document.addEventListener('alpine:init', () => Alpine.plugin(pluginFn))` instead of starting a second one.
- `@alpinejs/mask`'s `$money(input, delimiter, thousands, precision)` — verified from actual plugin source, not assumed: `delimiter` is the *decimal-point* character (defaults to `.`), not the thousands separator; `thousands` is the actual character inserted every 3 digits; get the argument order wrong and values silently land in the wrong slot rather than erroring. With `precision: 0`, feeding it a value that already contains a literal decimal point (e.g. straight from a `decimal:2`-cast Eloquent attribute, always `"30000.00"`) inflates the displayed number 100× — the mask strips the `.` as a non-digit and the trailing two decimal digits get absorbed as extra whole-number digits. Fix at the data source (cast to a whole-number string before binding), not by fighting the mask's config.
- Flux's `flux:input` component detects `.live` on the bound `wire:model` automatically and injects its own trailing loading spinner for free (`vendor/livewire/flux/stubs/.../input/index.blade.php`) — opt out per-field with `:loading="false"` if you're handling loading feedback yourself elsewhere (e.g. a scoped spinner in an adjacent cell via `wire:target`).
- **Why `purchase_order_receipts` exists as its own table, beyond "logging":** a single PO item can be fulfilled by multiple separate delivery events (e.g. 40 units Monday, 60 units Friday) — the receipts table is that event log, and `purchase_order_items.quantity_received` is *derived* from it (`SUM()` over receipts, recomputed, never incremented directly) — same "derived/cached field" pattern as `current_stock`/`total_amount`. This buys: (a) self-healing — the aggregate can't drift out of sync no matter how the underlying receipts change; (b) per-event accountability — each receipt has its own `received_by`/`received_at`, since different staff can receive different partial deliveries on different days; (c) it's the natural transaction boundary `ReceivePurchaseOrderAction` hangs one `CreateStockMovementAction` call on, one per receipt; (d) it's what makes FIFO lot-tracking possible later — a `stock_lot` is naturally one-per-receipt-event, not one-per-PO-item, since FIFO ordering is defined by *when each batch arrived*, not by PO creation time.
- **"Self-healing" describes the aggregate, not a license to edit receipt rows in place.** There's a real fork in how to "fix a wrong receipt": (1) edit the receipt row directly — simpler UI, but silently rewrites history (no record that 100 was originally claimed and later corrected to 85), which undermines the accountability point above; (2) never edit, record a correction as a *new* entry (reversal/adjustment receipt referencing the original) — preserves the full history, same posture `stock_movements` already takes elsewhere (`const UPDATED_AT = null`, immutable by design). `purchase_order_receipts` currently has a plain `updated_at` via `$table->timestamps()` (unlike `stock_movements`), so the schema itself doesn't force either answer — genuinely undecided, flagged against the receive-PO UI item below.
- **How a `Route::livewire(...)` page gets wrapped in a layout, traced end to end (Session 10):** a Livewire full-page component's own template renders to a plain HTML string first (`$content`). `Livewire\Features\SupportPageComponents\SupportPageComponents::renderContentsIntoLayout()` then generates and evaluates `@component($layout->view) @slot($layout->slotOrSection) {!! $content !!} @endslot @endcomponent` around it — `$layout->view` defaults to `config('livewire.component_layout')`, `$layout->slotOrSection` defaults to the literal string `'slot'` (Blade's convention for the *default* slot). So `$content` becomes the layout component's `$slot` variable with zero manual wiring in the page file itself. In this app: `components/layouts/app.blade.php` receives that as its `$slot`, wraps it in `<flux:main>`, and passes *that wrapped result* down as a new, separately-scoped `$slot` to `<x-layouts.app.sidebar>` — each component invocation gets its own local `$slot`, they are not the same variable just because they share a name. `sidebar.blade.php` then echoes its own `$slot` directly into `<body>`, next to the chrome it builds itself.
- **`Route::view(...)` pages vs. `Route::livewire(...)` pages — only the latter gets auto-wrapped.** `dashboard.blade.php` and `dashboards/placeholder.blade.php` are registered via plain `Route::view(...)` — no Livewire component involved at all, so `config('livewire.component_layout')` never applies to them; that's exactly why both explicitly self-wrap with `<x-layouts.app>` in their own template. Same story for the Fortify-resolved auth pages (login/register/etc.) — Fortify renders them as plain views too, so they also self-wrap (`<x-layouts.guest>`/`<x-layouts.auth>`). Only components registered via `Route::livewire(...)` (Create/List/View PO, the 3 Settings pages) rely on the config default, and a given component can still opt out per-instance via `#[Layout('some.other.view')]`.
- **`config('livewire.component_layout')`'s default (`'layouts::app'`) is a *namespaced view lookup*, not a component tag** — and it depends on a `Blade::anonymousComponentPath()` call existing somewhere, since that call is what registers the namespace. This is why Laravel's official Livewire starter kit ships that line as boilerplate; it's not app-specific config, Livewire's own package genuinely expects it. Removing it without also repointing `component_layout` breaks every `Route::livewire()` page silently (no error until an actual request/render is attempted) — see Session 10 above for the concrete break-and-fix.
- **Laravel service-provider registration order, and why `AppServiceProvider`'s `LoginResponseContract` override actually wins:** traced in `Illuminate\Foundation\Application::registerConfiguredProviders()` — providers register in this order: framework (`Illuminate\*`) providers first, then all Composer-auto-discovered **package** providers (this is where vendor's `Laravel\Fortify\FortifyServiceProvider` runs, which itself does `$this->app->singleton(LoginResponseContract::class, LoginResponse::class)` bound to *Fortify's own* default response), then finally the app's own `bootstrap/providers.php` list in file order (`AppServiceProvider`, then this project's `App\Providers\FortifyServiceProvider`). A container `singleton()` call doesn't stack — the second registration for the same abstract simply overwrites the first — so because the app's `AppServiceProvider::register()` runs *after* the vendor Fortify provider, its binding to the app's own `App\Http\Responses\LoginResponse` (role-based dashboard redirect) is the one still active when `AuthenticatedSessionController::store()` later calls `app(LoginResponse::class)`.
- **`'auth'` in `Route::middleware(['auth'])` is a Laravel built-in default middleware alias**, not something this project defines — resolves to `Illuminate\Auth\Middleware\Authenticate::class`, registered by the framework itself (`Illuminate\Foundation\Configuration\Middleware.php`). This project's `bootstrap/app.php` only *adds* `dashboard.access` to the same alias array; it never touches `auth`. Behavior: checks `Auth::guard(...)->check()`; if unauthenticated, throws `AuthenticationException` → Laravel's handler redirects to the `login` named route for a normal browser request, or returns 401 for a JSON request.
- **Parameterized route → Eloquent model binding for Livewire page components** works via `Livewire\Drawer\ImplicitRouteBinding`, which the class's own docblock says "mirrors the functionality of Laravel's `Illuminate\Routing\ImplicitRouteBinding`" (Livewire needs its own copy since a page component isn't a normal controller action, so the framework's own `SubstituteBindings` middleware doesn't cover it). **The match is by variable/property *name*, not by model class name** — proven from the actual matching code, `getPublicPropertyTypes($component)->intersectByKeys($route->parametersWithoutNulls())`: this intersects on array *keys* (names), the class name just rides along as the value for later. So `/purchasing/orders/{purchaseOrder}` binds to `public PurchaseOrder $purchaseOrder` because the *names* match (`purchaseOrder` ↔ `purchaseOrder`); the `PurchaseOrder` type hint only tells it *which class* to query once a name-match is found — swapping the route segment to `{order}` while keeping the property named `$purchaseOrder` would silently stop the binding, even though the type hint is unchanged. The actual DB lookup is Eloquent's own `resolveRouteBinding()` (effectively `PurchaseOrder::where('id', $value)->first()`, using the model's route key — `id` here, `PurchaseOrder` doesn't override `getRouteKeyName()`); a non-match throws `ModelNotFoundException`, which becomes a 404 *before* the component's `mount()` body ever runs — no manual `findOrFail()` needed. Resolution happens twice off one merged params array (once for `mount()`'s arguments, once for matching public properties), which is why `$this->purchaseOrder` is already the same resolved object by the time `mount()`'s first line executes.
- **`Model::load([...])` is Eloquent's "lazy eager loading"** (Laravel's own docblock: "Eager load relations on the model") — distinct from both **lazy loading** (touching an un-preloaded relation on the fly, e.g. `$po->supplier` with nothing pre-loaded — the N+1 risk when done inside a loop) and plain **eager loading** (`::with([...])` declared at the original query). `load()` is "lazy" only in *timing* — called after you already have the model instance (e.g. from route-model binding) — but "eager" in *behavior*: it batches one dedicated query per named relation (including one more per dot-nested level, e.g. `items.product`), not one query per access. This is why `$purchaseOrder->load(['supplier', 'createdBy', 'approvedBy', 'items.product'])` in View PO's `mount()` exists — it front-loads everything the template will touch in a fixed, small number of queries regardless of how many line items the PO has.

- **Taught 2026-10-23 (no code): overhead + inventory account concepts.**
  - **BOP Dibebankan vs BOP Sesungguhnya** — same factory overhead measured at two moments: *dibebankan* = estimated, charged into products at posting (machine hours × tarif; Cr BOP Dibebankan, a contra-expense with credit balance); *sesungguhnya* = the real bills (Dr BOP Listrik / Penyusutan / … ). Month-end closing compares them: actual > applied = under-applied (gap added to HPP), applied > actual = over-applied (gap reduces HPP); both go to 0. A consistently large gap means the rate is off.
  - **BOP Listrik** (and the other 4 BOP accounts) = **Beban**, debit, temporary — holds the month's actual cost until the closing moves it into products/HPP via BOP Dibebankan + the closing difference. It becomes part of *harga pokok*, but the account itself is Beban (code 5-…).
  - **Persediaan Barang Dalam Proses (WIP) vs Persediaan Barang Setengah Jadi** — WIP = **cost** accumulating on work orders still in production (not countable stock; zero once the WO's output is received). Setengah Jadi = **finished components on the warehouse shelf** (countable lots, e.g. 145 Badan Termos), waiting to be issued to the next stage (assembly). Flow for one Badan: issue PP → Dr WIP; result → Dr WIP (labour + overhead); Gudang receives → Dr Setengah Jadi / Cr WIP; assembly takes Badan → Dr WIP / Cr Setengah Jadi; finished Termos received → Dr Barang Jadi / Cr WIP. Kept separate (user's choice) so WIP really means "cost tied up in production now" and Setengah Jadi matches the WIP rows of Laporan Persediaan Gudang.
- **Taught 2026-10-21 (no code): purchase-invoice concepts.**
  - **Biaya persediaan on the faktur** = this receipt's share of the PO charges ticked "masuk biaya persediaan" (landed cost, Session 26). E.g. PO 100 kg × 30.000 + freight 200.000 → 2.000/kg; 40 kg received → lot 32.000/kg, receipt journal credits Barang Diterima Belum Difakturkan 1.280.000; pre-fill = 1.280.000 − 40 × 30.000 = **80.000** (`defaultCapitalizedCharges()` = Σ received value − qty × PO price). It must be on the faktur so the account clears to exactly 0; editable — if the supplier bills a different freight amount, the gap goes to Selisih Harga Pembelian. Unticked charges (admin, materai) go to Biaya Lainnya → expense account instead.
  - **Jatuh tempo** — typed by Akuntansi from the supplier's faktur (now starts empty, required, ≥ invoice date); used only for the overdue badge/count. Not computed because suppliers have no payment terms (decided: keep manual).
  - **Flow order** — strictly receipt → faktur → payment; no down payments (decided).
  - **PPN Masukan** (input VAT) = PPN paid to suppliers; a **Harta** (asset, debit) — a claim against the state, because PT SAI is a PKP: each month PPN Keluaran (charged on sales, owed to the state) − PPN Masukan = **kurang bayar** paid to the state (or **lebih bayar**, carried forward / refunded). Never inventory cost / HPP, since it's recovered. Only creditable with a valid supplier faktur pajak — the faktur pajak number was removed from the app (user), so that lives in e-Faktur / Coretax; the app's PPN Masukan balance assumes all supplier PPN is creditable.
  - **The PPN journal, step by step** (example 100 kg × 30.000, PPN 11%): (1) receipt — Dr Persediaan 3.000.000 / Cr Barang Diterima Belum Difakturkan 3.000.000 (no PPN yet: PPN comes from the faktur); (2) faktur — Dr Barang Diterima Belum Difakturkan 3.000.000 + **Dr PPN Masukan 330.000** / Cr Utang Usaha 3.330.000 (inventory stays 3.000.000); (3) payment — Dr Utang Usaha 3.330.000 / Cr Kas-Bank; (4) **month-end offset (not built — needs Sales Invoice for PPN Keluaran)** — e.g. Dr PPN Keluaran 550.000 / Cr PPN Masukan 330.000, Cr **Utang PPN** 220.000, then Dr Utang PPN / Cr Kas when paid to the state. Net effect: the 330.000 paid to the supplier is 330.000 less paid to the state, so the tax costs nothing. Steps 1–3 are built and automatic; until step 4 exists the PPN Masukan balance only grows (correct, just not offset). Step 4 will need new system roles (PPN Keluaran, Utang PPN) — part of the Sales Invoice / tax work.
- **Taught 2026-10-20: "Terendah – tertinggi" column (Laporan Biaya Produksi, summary per product)** — the range of HPP per unit across that product's WOs in the month. The average hides spread: e.g. 6.200 / 6.300 / 7.400 → average 6.633 but range 6.200–7.400 means one batch cost 1.200/unit more → look at that WO (more rejects, more labor/machine hours, a pricier FIFO material lot, a smaller batch). Narrow range = consistent production cost; wide = unstable process. Shows "—" when a product has only one WO in the month (true for every product in Oct 2026 dev data).
- **Taught 2026-10-20 (no code): Kerugian Bahan vs Kerugian Produk** (the Losses tile's "Bahan · Produk" split). **Kerugian Bahan = Selisih Bahan** account: raw-material *leftovers* that go missing — detected at **Retur Bahan** (reported leftover vs what Gudang counts back); can be **negative** (surplus). **Kerugian Produk = Kerugian Produksi** account: *finished units* that go missing — detected at **Penerimaan Produksi** (reported good units vs what Gudang counts in); **never negative** (receipt ≤ good). Different causes → different place to look: floor material handling vs the finished-goods handover. **Neither is a reject** — rejects are counted in the result and absorbed into the good units' cost; losses are things that should have reached the warehouse and didn't, so they're a separate expense.
- **Taught 2026-10-20: when "Perlu Perhatian" (production dashboard) shows warnings** — `ProductionKpis::attention()`, **any count > 0 triggers it (no age/size threshold)**, "right now" (ignores the period filter and the Barang Jadi / WIP toggle), and each item clears itself once the underlying task is done (nothing to dismiss). Five checks: 🔴 **red** — WO Released / In Progress with `planned_end_date` before today (link to WO list + up to 6 WO chips); 🟠 **amber** (Produksi can act now) — WO In Progress with no production result yet; production result still Draft (both link to Hasil Produksi); ⚪ **gray** (waiting on Gudang, **no link** — Produksi can't open warehouse pages) — posted result with no posted Penerimaan Produksi; posted result with unused issued material and no posted Retur Bahan (same leftover rule as the Material Return list). Green "Tidak ada yang perlu ditindaklanjuti" = all five are zero (true in the dev data on 2026-10-20).
- **Taught 2026-10-20 (no code): account types of the system-role accounts.** Barang Diterima Belum Difakturkan = Kewajiban Lancar (debt exists at receipt, only the invoice is missing; clears to Utang Usaha at invoice); Utang Gaji = Kewajiban Lancar (accrual: labor cost recorded when work is done, cleared by Dr Utang Gaji / Cr Kas at payday — no payroll feature yet, so a manual Jurnal Umum entry until then); BOP Dibebankan = contra-expense with credit balance (see Next Steps #45). Also explained: the Jurnal Umum footer total is a debit = credit check, not an account balance (reversal pairs are counted; they net out per account in Buku Besar / Neraca Saldo, not built yet); PO Biaya Lainnya vs PSAK 14 inventory cost (→ #44, built).

## Product Roadmap — managerial decision support (proposed 2026-10-20; progress as of 2026-10-21)

**Progress:** Layer 3 — **Kepala Produksi dashboard with charts ✅** and **production reports ✅** (Laporan Biaya Produksi, Laporan Persediaan Gudang; the Laporan grid page). Layer 1 — **Faktur Pembelian + Pembayaran Pembelian ✅** (Session 27; no down payments by decision). Layer 1 — **Biaya Overhead Aktual + month-end closing ✅** (Session 28). **Layer 1 Sales Invoice + customer payments ✅ (Session 31).** Still open: Layer 1 wage payments, monthly PPN offset (PPN Keluaran now exists); Layer 2 entirely; Layer 3 Manager / other role dashboards, Kebutuhan Bahan widget. **Permintaan Pembelian ✅** (Session 30, standalone with shortage suggestions).


**Goal (user, 2026-10-20):** the system's end goal is to give managers a better understanding of the company's financial state and support informed decisions. Features below are *beyond* what's built; layers depend on the previous one. Not committed to — order is the assistant's recommendation, user has not picked a first item yet.

**Layer 1 — finish the accounting cycle** (books currently stop at HPP; no report is complete without these)
- **Sales Invoice + customer payments** (Piutang Usaha, Pendapatan Penjualan, PPN Keluaran) — no revenue is recorded anywhere yet.
- **Purchase Invoice + supplier payments** (Utang Usaha, PPN Masukan) — clears Barang Diterima Belum Difakturkan; also where non-capitalised PO charges (`include_in_cost = false`) get expensed.
- **Biaya Overhead Aktual + month-end closing** (Next Steps #41) — BOP Sesungguhnya vs BOP Dibebankan, Selisih BOP → HPP.
- **Wage payments** — clears Utang Gaji; could start as a simple "bayar gaji" form, not a full payroll module.
- Adds the invoice-only `AccountSystemRole`s left out in Session 25 (`accounts_payable`, `vat_in`, `sales_revenue`, …).

**Layer 2 — standard financial reports** (all read `journal_entry_lines`, grouped differently; decide Next Steps #45 contra accounts first)
- **Buku Besar** (lines per account + running balance) → **Neraca Saldo** (ending balance per account) → **Laba Rugi** → **Neraca** → **Arus Kas** (profit ≠ cash).

**Layer 3 — managerial reports** (the "why", not just the "what")
| Report | Decision it supports | Data ready? |
|---|---|---|
| Work Order cost plan vs actual (material / labor / overhead / unit cost per WO) | which products/batches overrun, on which element | ✅ since Sessions 17–19 |
| Product unit cost trend over time | is a product getting costlier → pricing | ✅ production results |
| Inventory valuation + aging (value per product, lot age) | cash tied up in stock, slow-moving material | ✅ `inventory_lots` |
| Losses & variances (Selisih Bahan, Kerugian Produksi, reject rate per product / work center) | where waste/shrinkage happens | ✅ returns, receipts, results |
| Estimated gross margin per product / customer (SO price vs FIFO HPP at delivery) | which products/customers really earn money | ✅ estimate until invoices exist |
| Overhead rate accuracy (applied vs actual per month) | is the rate realistic → product cost bias | needs #41 |
| Piutang / utang aging | whom to chase, what's due | needs Layer 1 |
| Manager dashboard (sales, gross margin, stock value, overdue AR, open WOs) | daily snapshot | combines the above |

**Layer 3 format + access — decided (user, 2026-10-20):**
- **Both a dashboard and report pages.** Dashboard = a few KPIs, small trends, alerts, each linking to its report; lives in the **existing per-role dashboards** (`view-dashboard` gate, currently "Coming soon" placeholders) — each role sees KPIs for the reports it may access. Report pages = full table, period/product filters, drill-down to WO/lot/product.
- **Export: PDF + CSV** (reports only, not the dashboard). PDF via the already-installed `barryvdh/laravel-dompdf` (same setup as the PO PDF); CSV with no new package. No `.xlsx` / `maatwebsite/excel`.
- **Access per report (least privilege):**

| Report | Manager | Akuntansi | Kepala Produksi | Sales | Gudang |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| WO plan vs actual (cost) | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | | |
| Losses & variances, reject rate | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | | |
| Product unit cost trend | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | |
| Inventory valuation (Rp) | ✅ | ✅ | | | |
| Inventory aging (quantity only) | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | | ✅ |
| Gross margin per product / customer | ✅ | ✅ | | ✅ | |
| Piutang aging | ✅ | ✅ | | ✅ | |
| Utang aging | ✅ | ✅ | | | |
| Financial statements (Layer 2) | ✅ | ✅ | | | |

  Reasoning: Kepala Produksi gets the production reports because they're the one who can act on them, and they already see per-WO cost on Hasil Produksi; they don't get margin / customer / company-wide value. **Sales sees gross margin** (user: yes — pricing/discount decisions; they already have `costs.view` since Session 22) **and piutang aging** (user: yes — Sales chases customer payments); not utang aging. Implement as new per-report gates (e.g. `reports.production`, `reports.margin`, `reports.receivables`, …) rather than reusing `costs.view`, so each report's audience can change independently. Purchasing / System Admin not given any Layer 3 report — not discussed; ask if it comes up.

**Production dashboard charts — being designed (2026-10-20), not built yet.** User wants charts on the Kepala Produksi dashboard; discussing what data first. Candidate set proposed (6 visuals, one per question): output per week · reject rate trend 6 months · plan vs actual cost per WO (diverging bar) · work center utilisation (actual machine hours ÷ `overhead_rates.budgeted_machine_hours`, which is a **monthly** budget) · on-time rate (`production_date` ≤ `planned_end_date`) · losses trend. Reject *reasons* can't be charted — `reject_reason` is free text (would need a fixed list).
- **Decided — output chart:** **per week**, **stacked bar by product** (top 5 + "Lainnya"), clicking a product in the legend isolates it and **then shows that product's target line** (target only meaningful per product), plus a **Barang Jadi / WIP toggle**. Why not an overall total: products differ in kind, and WIP components (Badan, Tutup, Handle) assembled into a Termos would be counted several times.
- **Decided — the other 5 (user: "ok all five") — built, see Session 26:** (2) reject rate trend 6 months, line, overall + per product on legend click; per-product bars stay. (3) plan vs actual cost per WO, diverging bar (over = right/amber, under = left/green), top 10 by difference, Rp labor + overhead, material-over-plan marker kept. (4) work center utilisation, progress bar per work center = actual machine hours in the month ÷ `budgeted_machine_hours` (monthly). (5) on-time rate, tile + small donut, overall, % completed on/before `planned_end_date` + late count. (6) losses trend 6 months, stacked bar, Selisih Bahan + Kerugian Produksi.
- **Decided — Barang Jadi / WIP toggle is page-level** (applies to output, reject, plan vs actual; work center utilisation exempt — machines aren't split by product type).
- Output-chart target: each WO's `quantity_target` counted in the week of its `planned_end_date`; good units in the week of the result's `production_date`.

**Kebutuhan Bahan + Permintaan Pembelian (PR) — decided 2026-10-20, both built LATER (after the dashboard charts).**
- **Kebutuhan Bahan widget** (Kepala Produksi dashboard; simple MRP): per material — **needed** = Σ `work_order_materials.quantity_planned` − already issued, over WOs in Draft / Released / In Progress · **in stock** = `products.current_stock` · **on order** = open POs (Approved / Partially Received) quantity not yet received · **shortfall** = needed − stock − on order, highlighted, with a "Buat Permintaan Pembelian" button once PRs exist. Plain stock levels alone aren't useful to production — only stock vs upcoming need.
- **Permintaan Pembelian (Purchase Requisition):** the requester says what / how much / by when — **no supplier or price** (Purchasing's job). Flow: Produksi → PR → Purchasing reviews, turns one or more PRs into a PO (can combine PRs for the same material) → supplier. Decisions (user): (1) **only Produksi creates PRs** (not Gudang; Gudang's reorder-point path #15/#36 stays separate); (2) **no approval step** — PR goes straight to Purchasing, who decides; (3) **PR records which WOs it's for**, so Purchasing sees the reason and the deadline (earliest planned start date of those WOs); (4) build **later**. Not in the ERD yet (new tables needed, e.g. `purchase_requisitions` + `purchase_requisition_items` + a PR↔WO link; PO should remember which PR(s) it came from). Use case diagram: Pending Changes #10.

**⚠️ SUPERSEDED the same day — see "Production reports, final" below.** ~~Production reports — decided 2026-10-20 (user wants only two, to keep the thesis scope small).** Both: report page with filters + **PDF + CSV** export; access Produksi, Manager, Akuntansi (own gate).
1. **Laporan Kebutuhan Bahan** (material requirements, simple MRP; the full version of the planned Kebutuhan Bahan widget) — **as of today**, not per period. Per raw material: needed (planned − already issued, over WOs Draft / Released / In Progress) · in stock (`current_stock`) · on order (open POs Approved / Partially Received, not yet received) · shortfall = needed − stock − on order (highlighted > 0) · earliest needed-by (earliest planned start of the WOs needing it) · below-reorder-point flag. Expandable to which WOs need it. **Draft WOs are included but marked separately** ("rencana belum dirilis") — user decision.
2. **Laporan Realisasi Produksi** (the "most important" one — the job order costing / *Kartu Harga Pokok Pesanan* view, since each WO is a job) — per period, one row per completed WO: output (target, good, reject, reject %, achieved %), time (planned end vs completion, on time/late), material cost (+ over plan by qty), labor planned vs actual hours & cost, overhead planned vs actual machine hours & cost, total cost, unit cost (HPP/unit), difference vs plan; period total row; WO links to its detail. Rejected alternatives: Laporan Harga Pokok Produksi (opening WIP + costs − closing WIP) — that's an Akuntansi / Layer 2 report built from the ledger; a separate reject/efficiency report — already a column group here.

Already planned elsewhere, purchasing-side rather than financial: safety stock / reorder point calculation (#36), low-stock notification (#15).

**Recommended order:** (1) Layer-3 quick wins on existing data — WO plan vs actual + inventory valuation (useful now, strong thesis material showing managerial insight) → (2) Layer 1: Purchase Invoice → Sales Invoice → Overhead Aktual + closing → (3) Layer 2: Buku Besar + Neraca Saldo, then Laba Rugi + Neraca → (4) remaining Layer 3 + dashboard.

   d. ~~**Production Results**~~ **Built (2026-10-09)** — see the build note at the end of this item. Original plan: actual good/reject, worker hours (pre-filled from `work_order_labors`), machine hours, materials; material + labor + overhead (machine hours × copied rate) → `unit_cost`; who submits (paper log typed in by planner/supervisor?) and who verifies — open.
      **Decided 2026-10-08:**
      - **One production result per WO.** Production staff report only once the work is completed, so recording the result closes the WO's production. Each issue line's `quantity_consumed` is set once, at that moment.
      - **Material used = reported by production staff (actual).** The paper report states how much of each material was used; it is typed into the result form. It is not derived from formula × output.
      - **Consumption across issue lines = FIFO by issue.** When a WO has several posted issues of the same material, the oldest issue line is consumed first. Cost charged to the WO = Σ issue line `total_cost` − value of the Material Return, where the returned value is priced at the refilled lots' movement costs (see below). It is **not** consumed qty × the line's average `unitCost()`: the return reverses specific FIFO draws, so the remaining cost must be the exact rest.
      - **Leftovers (issued − consumed) go back to the warehouse via a Material Return document** (Retur Bahan). It is an IN stock movement, returned at the same cost it left at (traceable through the issue line's OUT movements), and taken off the WO's cost. Because the WO closes with its single result, leftovers must be settled at result time.
      - **Material Return is a separate document posted by Gudang** (decided 2026-10-08). Production records the result, including the leftover per material. Stock increases only when Gudang posts the return, confirming the material physically arrived. The WO therefore needs a "waiting for return" step after the result before it is fully closed (not modelled yet; decide the status or flag when building).
      - **Returned stock goes back into the original lots** (decided 2026-10-08). The IN movements reverse the issue line's OUT movements: per lot, add back `quantity_remaining` and `value_remaining` at that lot's movement cost, so FIFO order is preserved. An emptied lot can become non-empty again. **Partial return refills last-drawn first** (reverse of the draw order, decided 2026-10-08). Example: 45 kg drawn as LOT-012 30 kg then LOT-013 15 kg; a 2 kg return goes into LOT-013 at its cost. The resulting stock looks exactly as if only 43 kg had been drawn by FIFO. Each lot can be refilled by at most what was drawn from it for that line.
      **✅ ERD updated to match (2026-10-08, now 45 tables):** `production_results.work_order_id` one-to-one (`ref: -`, unique `(work_order_id)`; `production_date` = completion date; `shift` kept); `production_result_materials.quantity` = actual reported usage, allocated FIFO to issue lines, `total_cost` = line value − leftover value priced at the last-drawn lots; `production_results.material_cost` = issued − returned; `material_issue_items.quantity_consumed` note; new **`material_returns`** (`return_number`, `work_order_id`, `production_result_id`, `created_by`, `received_by`/`received_at` set on posting, `status document_status`) + **`material_return_items`** (`material_issue_item_id`, `product_id`, `quantity`, `total_cost`; unique (return, issue item)), in the Produksi TableGroup; `stock_movement_type` gained **`material_return`**. Morph key for the IN movements when built: `material_return_item`. Not in the ERD (still open): the WO "waiting for return" status/flag, and the Gudang use case name.
      **Decided 2026-10-09 (ERD updated):**
      - **Kepala Produksi enters the result; no approval step.** They are responsible for the production amounts. ERD: `production_result_status` enum and `verified_by`/`verified_at`/`rejection_reason`/`submitted_*` removed; `status document_status` (Draft → Posted, same pattern as Material Issue; the draft step is an assumption, can be dropped), `created_by`, `posted_by`/`posted_at`. Journal on posting.
      - **One reject type, cost absorbed by good units.** No normal/abnormal split. ERD: `reject_type` enum + column, `abnormal_loss_cost`, `output_cost` removed; `unit_cost` = `total_cost` ÷ `quantity_good`; `spoilage_loss` dropped from the account keys; journal no longer debits Kerugian Produk Rusak.
      - **No "waiting for return" WO status.** A Material Return is only inventory coming back; the WO doesn't wait for it.
      - **Return quantity differs from reported leftover → record the actual amount received; the difference is a loss/gain, not WO cost** (Option B). The WO's cost stays as posted. ERD: `material_return_items.quantity_expected` (reported leftover) + `quantity` (actual) + `variance_value`; journal only for the difference (issues have no journal, so the reported leftover's value is still in the inventory account): shortfall Dr Selisih Bahan / Cr Persediaan, surplus the reverse. New account key `material_variance`.

      **Build (2026-10-09), decisions taken just before building:** WO becomes **Completed** when the result is posted, whatever the good quantity (92/100 = a batch of 92) — **`completed_short` removed** from `WorkOrderStatus` (In Progress → Completed only) and `work_orders.closed_by/closed_at` dropped (migration `production/2026_10_09_100100_remove_close_fields_from_work_orders.php`, refuses if any completed_short row exists); Released → In Progress stays on the first posted issue (confirmed). Workers: planned rows + **extra workers can be added** (rate = their labor rate on the completion date; must be active with a rate). Material used **pre-filled with the issued quantity**. **No shift**, only the completion date. Plan vs actual always shown side by side.

      - Models `ProductionResult` (`usedQuantities()`, `transitionTo`, `ensureIsDraft`), `ProductionResultMaterial`, `ProductionResultLabor`; `WorkOrder::productionResult()` (HasOne), `canRecordResult()` (In Progress), `postedIssueItems($lock)` (posted lines, oldest issue first). **`MaterialIssueItem::leftoverValue($qty)`** — walks the line's OUT movements newest first (whole draw = its `total_value`, partial = qty × unit cost); reused later by Material Return.
      - DTO `ProductionResultData`. Actions: **`ValidateProductionResultAction`** (good > 0; date ≤ today and ≥ first posted issue date; used ≤ issued per product; ≥1 worker with hours; added workers active + rate on the date) — shared by save and post; `SaveProductionResultDraftAction` (one per WO, In Progress only; allocates used per product to issue lines oldest first via static `allocate()`); **`PostProductionResultAction`** (locks result + WO; blocks if a material issue draft is open; re-validates; re-allocates over locked issue lines; line cost = line `total_cost` − `leftoverValue(leftover)`; sets `quantity_consumed`; labor at the WO's copied rate or the added worker's rate on the date; overhead = machine hours × WO `overhead_rate`; unit cost = total ÷ good; posts; WO `quantity_good/reject` + **Completed**). Exceptions `InvalidProductionResultException`, `WorkOrderNotRecordableException`, `ProductionResultNotEditableException`. `ProductionResultPolicy` (view = `production.view`; create/update/post = `production.create` + draft). No journal yet (no accounting tables).
      - Pages `resources/views/pages/production/results/`: `⚡list` (tab 1 WOs In Progress with "Catat hasil" / "Buka draf"; tab 2 result documents), `⚡form` (create `/create/{workOrder}` — redirects to the existing result if one exists — and edit; output with planned target, machine hours with planned, workers planned vs actual + "Tambah pekerja", materials planned / issued / used / leftover), `⚡show` (plan vs actual for output, machine, workers (difference, rate, cost), materials (vs plan, leftover, cost); cost summary with planned labor/overhead; Post modal). WO detail page: "Catat hasil" (In Progress, no result) or "Hasil Produksi" link. Sidebar Produksi → "Hasil Produksi". Routes `production.results.*`. 49 translation keys (`lang/id.json` 458).
      - **Verified (rolled back):** WO-2026-000015, 1.5 kg PP Merah leftover + an added worker → material 1.227.000 (1.272.000 − 1.5 × 30.000), labor 427.000, overhead 232.500, total 1.886.500, unit 6.505,17 for 290 good; issued cost = used cost + leftover value; WO Completed; a second result refused / create redirects; warehouse user gets 403. ERD updated (no `completed_short`, no WO `closed_*`, no `shift`, labor notes + unique). Visual layout not verified — check in the browser. 44. ~~**PO "Biaya Lainnya" never reaches inventory cost**~~ **✅ Decided + built 2026-10-20: option A (see Session 26).** ~~Original:~~ `ReceivePurchaseOrderAction` values each lot as `quantity × unit_price` only (lines ~44–45) and journals exactly that (Dr InventoryRaw / Cr GoodsReceivedNotInvoiced, ~73–74); `purchase_order_charges` only affect `purchase_orders.total_amount` + the supplier PDF. Per PSAK 14, costs needed to bring goods to the factory (ongkos angkut pembelian, bongkar muat, asuransi pengiriman, bea masuk) belong in *biaya perolehan* persediaan, so if charges are mostly freight, FIFO lot cost → material issue → production cost → HPP are all understated; non-delivery fees (admin, materai) are correctly expenses. **Options to decide:** (A) allocate charges into lot unit cost at receipt — needs an allocation basis (by value share of subtotal, or qty/weight), proportional handling of partial receipts, and a per-charge flag/type for "masuk HPP" vs beban; (B) leave receipts as is and handle charges when the invoice arrives — Dr Biaya Angkut Pembelian (expense) or an inventory adjustment; simpler, but lots already issued to production never get the extra cost. **Natural time to decide: when building Purchase Invoice** (that's where actual charge amounts are confirmed and GRNI clears).
45. **Open (2026-10-20, not decided): COA can't represent contra accounts.** `AccountType::normalBalance()` forces the normal balance from the category (Harta/Beban = Debit; Kewajiban/Modal/Pendapatan = Kredit), and the account form doesn't let the user pick it. Contra accounts need the *opposite* side of their group: **BOP Dibebankan** (`OverheadApplied` role — contra-expense, credit; cleared at month-end against BOP Sesungguhnya, difference = Selisih BOP → HPP), later **Akumulasi Penyusutan** (contra-asset, credit) and **Retur/Potongan Penjualan** (contra-revenue, debit). **Workaround for now:** create BOP Dibebankan under **Beban**; posting is unaffected (journals never check normal balance), it just carries a credit balance inside the expense group. **Where it will matter:** Buku Besar / Neraca Saldo / financial statements will use normal balance to sign balances, so contra accounts would show as "negative". **Usual fix:** an `is_contra` toggle on `accounts` that flips the category's normal balance (keeps the category for statement grouping). Decide before building the ledger/trial balance.

25. **2FA-completed logins skip the role-based dashboard redirect (found Session 10, not fixed).** `AuthenticatedSessionController::store()` (plain password login) resolves `LoginResponseContract`, which this app overrides in `AppServiceProvider`. `TwoFactorAuthenticatedSessionController::store()` resolves a *different* contract, `TwoFactorLoginResponse`, which nothing in `app/` overrides — a 2FA user currently falls through to Fortify's generic `redirect()->intended(Fortify::redirects('login'))` instead of their role dashboard. Low priority until real 2FA users exist, but a one-line fix when it matters (mirror the existing `LoginResponse` override for this second contract).

19. ~~Timezone~~ **Fixed (2026-10-08).** `config/app.php` → `'timezone' => env('APP_TIMEZONE', 'Asia/Jakarta')` (WIB, UTC+7 — the factory is in Surabaya); `config/database.php` mysql → `'timezone' => env('DB_TIMEZONE', '+07:00')` so TIMESTAMP columns convert correctly on any server (before, it only worked because the laptop's MySQL system zone happened to be +7 / "Asia/Bangkok"); both keys added to `.env` and `.env.example`. Existing test data was 7 h early (Laravel wrote UTC strings that MySQL read as +7) → shifted every timestamp/datetime column +7 h with a one-off tinker script (45 columns scanned, 37 with data, one transaction; deliberately **not** a migration so it can never run against a real database). Verified: `now()` = MySQL `NOW()` = wall clock; `CarbonImmutable` dead-code note below still stands. Original note, for history: `config/app.php:68` hardcoded `'timezone' => 'UTC'` (not read from `.env`). Every `now()` call project-wide (`created_at`, `approved_at`, and the soon-to-exist `received_at` in `ReceivePurchaseOrderAction`) is stamped in UTC, and nothing at display time converts to Indonesia local time (`Asia/Jakarta`, UTC+7) — Blade views just format whatever timezone the Carbon instance already carries. Every timestamp shown in the UI is currently ~7 hours behind local wall-clock time. Decide: convert at display time only (keep storing UTC — more standard practice), or change `app.timezone` to `Asia/Jakarta` outright. User explicitly deferred ("put a pin on that, circle back to it later"). **More urgent since Session 17:** "today"-based rules now exist (WO planned start ≥ today, PO delivery ≥ order date, date-picker `min`s) — between 00:00 and 07:00 WIB the server's UTC "today" is still yesterday; month-end overhead closing will also depend on it. Separately/relatedly: `AppServiceProvider::configureDefaults()` (which calls `Date::use(CarbonImmutable::class)`) is defined but never called from `boot()` — dead code, `now()` currently returns mutable `Carbon`, not `CarbonImmutable`.
